- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40693v1
- Title: Finite-Element Electromagnetic Analysis and Evaluation of Antenna Performance Metrics Using a Quantum Linear System Solver
- Authors: Hiroaki Ominato, Takahiro Ohyama, Shiro Kawabata
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.CE
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40693v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40693v1.pdf

Abstract:
Electromagnetic analysis based on the finite element method is widely used in antenna design. At higher frequencies, however, the required mesh refinement makes solving large sparse linear systems the main contributor to computational cost. Quantum linear system solvers offer a potential means of accelerating this step, but their output is a superposition state: reconstructing all components of the classical solution vector would forfeit the quantum speedup. Prior studies have proposed methods for obtaining physical quantities such as scattering cross sections and scattering parameters from the solution state. To support antenna design using fault-tolerant quantum computers, we formulate the total radiated power, the radiated power within a prescribed solid angle, and the dielectric and conductive losses as observables for efficient evaluation from a quantum solution state. These observables avoid the measurement cost of reconstructing all components of the output quantum state. We develop an end-to-end hybrid quantum--classical workflow that combines finite-element electromagnetic analysis, matrix preconditioning, a quantum linear system solver, and the evaluation of antenna design metrics. To validate the workflow, we performed numerical experiments evaluating these observables for an axisymmetric coaxially fed monopole antenna. Under idealized conditions, the relative errors in the total radiated power and the dissipated power with respect to the classical finite-element solution were on the order of $10^{-3}$, confirming the consistency of the workflow. The proposed method is expected to provide a basis for using quantum linear system solvers to evaluate antenna performance and, in the future, to design high-frequency wireless devices.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40709v1
- Title: Finite-resource cooling of qudits beyond thermodynamic length
- Authors: Ansh Mishra, Aryan Senthilkumar
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40709v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40709v1.pdf

Abstract:
Cooling a quantum system with $N$ thermal machines costs more than Landauer's bound. For swap-based collision models the excess is set by thermodynamic length, which is optimal for qubits and was suggested by numerics to be optimal in general. We show that for qudits, permutations of energy eigenstates that use several resonant transitions at once do better for every spectrum, reducing the excess by up to 83% near the ground state. To second order, coherent collisions do no better, and at infinite temperature $d_M$-level machines reduce the excess by at most a factor $d_M-1$.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40762v1
- Title: Quantum manifestations of converse symmetry breaking
- Authors: Daniel P. Buser, Biswabibek Bandyopadhyay, Adilson E. Motter
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.dis-nn
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40762v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40762v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum synchronization holds promise in advancing quantum technologies such as sensing, communication, and computation. A key challenge in this rapidly developing field is to identify mechanisms that can promote the emergence of synchronous dynamics. Here, we show that quantum synchronization can be enhanced by introducing controlled heterogeneity in the form of coupling mismatches. Using spin van der Pol oscillators as a model system, we show that this mechanism constitutes a quantum analog of the phenomenon of converse symmetry breaking previously identified in classical oscillator networks, where a synchronous state is stabilized by explicit symmetry breaking. We quantify how quantum effects modify this behavior by tuning the degree of quantumness through variations of the spin quantum number. These findings suggest that quantum synchronization can be enhanced in experiments by harnessing rather than suppressing heterogeneity.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40776v1
- Title: A Universal Approach to Hydrodynamics in Higher Dimensions via Tensor Networks
- Authors: Sajant Anand, Christopher D. White, Jack Kemp, Julia Wei, Norman Y. Yao, Michael P. Zaletel
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.str-el
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40776v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40776v1.pdf

Abstract:
Extracting late-time hydrodynamic transport properties directly from microscopic quantum models, especially in dimensions beyond one, is believed to be classically intractable. This owes, in part, to the extensive growth of entanglement in higher dimensions and often a mismatch between geometric and tensor network locality. Here, we extend the Density Matrix Truncation (DMT) algorithm to accurately and quantitatively predict high-temperature transport coefficients (e.g. diffusion constants) in up to three dimensions. Additionally, we demonstrate the ability to capture the slow crossover dynamics characteristic of weakly broken integrability. The intuition underlying our approach is to preserve the geometrically local information crucial for hydrodynamics throughout the time evolution, despite using a one-dimensional matrix product state ansatz for higher-dimensional calculations. Our work pushes the boundary of classical computability and opens the door to benchmarking the high-temperature, late-time dynamics of generic quantum simulators.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40805v1
- Title: Fock-space delocalization and its control in scarred-vs-ergodic dynamics
- Authors: Francesco Perciavalle
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.quant-gas; cond-mat.stat-mech
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40805v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40805v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum many-body scarred dynamics in Rydberg-atom arrays is characterized by revivals, in which the system repeatedly returns to its initial state despite its many-body complexity. But how extensively does the wave function explore the space of allowed configurations between revivals? We address this question by quantifying the fraction of configurations occupied during PXP dynamics initialized from the paradigmatic polarized and Néel states, which respectively exhibit thermalizing and scarred dynamics. Despite their striking revivals, dynamics from the Néel state spreads over exponentially many configurations, yet occupies a vanishing fraction of the full space in the thermodynamic limit. Dynamics from the polarized state, by contrast, occupies a finite fraction of the configuration space, close to anticoncentration and random-state statistics, although chiral symmetry limits this fraction to one third. A weak quasiperiodic field enables the polarized state to occupy half of the configuration space by lifting the constraint imposed by chiral symmetry. The Néel state, however, requires a stronger field to reach a comparable anticoncentrated regime. As the field increases further, the occupied fraction decreases for both initial states, revealing a nonmonotonic dependence of Fock-space delocalization on field strength without necessarily implying localization.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40808v1
- Title: Liouvillian spectral snapshots: A reduced-subspace approach for time-dependent and noisy open quantum systems
- Authors: Hans Christiansen, André Melo, Jens Paaske, Fabrizio Minganti
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40808v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40808v1.pdf

Abstract:
Driven-dissipative quantum devices are routinely operated with time-dependent drives and exposed to temporally correlated classical noise. When these perturbations vary on timescales comparable to the system's intrinsic dynamics, rotating-wave and Markovian approximations can fail, making effective static descriptions unreliable. As a result, accurately modeling systems in this regime often relies on simulating the full time-dependent master equation. This is particularly demanding for processes requiring long integration times, such as rare switching between metastable states, or for stochastic perturbations requiring averaging over many noise realizations. To address these challenges, we introduce Liouvillian spectral snapshots (LSS), a reduced-basis method that enables efficient simulation of such systems by projecting the master equation onto a low-dimensional subspace capturing their coarse-grained dynamics. LSS constructs this subspace by computing the slowly decaying, low-frequency eigenoperators of the Liouvillian at a few adaptively sampled perturbation values and compressing them into a common basis. This basis is built once and can be reused across noise realizations and perturbation parameters, reducing the cost of ensemble averages and parameter sweeps.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40810v1
- Title: Beyond the Kubo formula: unification of adiabatic and isothermal response in quantum systems
- Authors: Giuliano Chiriacò, Vasco Cavina
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.quant-gas; cond-mat.str-el
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40810v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40810v1.pdf

Abstract:
The static limit of the Kubo susceptibility describes the adiabatic response of an isolated quantum system, in which energy-level occupations remain unchanged, and generally differs from the isothermal susceptibility, where these occupations readjust to thermal equilibrium. In this work, we introduce a single dynamical framework in which the adiabatic and isothermal responses arise naturally as two distinct limiting regimes. This can be done by considering explicitly an open system version of the Kubo approach in which the dissipation is governed by a typical thermalization rate of the system. By exchanging the order of the static limit and of the vanishing thermalization rate limit, the protocol yields either the adiabatic or isothermal response. This protocol has a solid physical motivation in terms of the thermalization dynamics and is valid for generic systems. We apply it to the case of the magnetic response of the electron gas and reproduce the expected results.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40827v1
- Title: Witnessing and quantifying stellar rank with the Wigner function
- Authors: Oliver Hahn, Alex Maltesson, Varun Upreti, Marike Ellerbroek, Alessandro Ferraro, Saleh Rahimi-Keshari, Ulysse Chabaud, Giulia Ferrini
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40827v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40827v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantifying non-Gaussianity is essential for assessing the computational power of bosonic quantum systems. We introduce a framework to witness and quantify non-Gaussianity from displaced parity measurements, and we directly apply it to experimental data obtained with superconducting cavities. Our method estimates fidelities with target pure states from the displaced parity measurements, enabling certification of non-Gaussianity via the stellar rank. We further introduce refined witnesses computed by measuring the value of the Wigner function at a few points, providing efficient certification tools outperforming existing ones. Applying these techniques to Fock, binomial, cat, and GKP experimental data, we rigorously certify non-Gaussian resources and establish a stellar rank of $8$ for a GKP state, setting a new benchmark across all quantum platforms. Our methods enable a rigorous quantification of non-Gaussian quantum resources directly applicable to superconducting devices.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40840v1
- Title: Electronic-Structure-Informed Many-Body Encoding for Quantum Machine Learning
- Authors: Liangnuo Zhang, Tanner Culpitt, Qiwei Yin, Linghua Zhu, Xiaosong Li
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.chem-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40840v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40840v1.pdf

Abstract:
Data encoding is a central challenge in quantum machine learning (QML), as it determines how classical information is mapped onto quantum states and influences the expressive power and efficiency of parameterized quantum circuits. Here, we introduce a physics-informed quantum encoding strategy that exploits the intrinsic many-body structure of electronic systems to establish a direct correspondence between the body order of input descriptors and the locality of quantum gates. Specifically, $n$-body electronic descriptors obtained from a single mean-field electronic-structure calculation are encoded through $n$-qubit gates, yielding a systematic hierarchy of quantum circuits with increasing many-body expressivity. Using reduced-density-matrix descriptors, we construct quantum machine-learning models incorporating one- through four-body encodings and benchmark their performance in predicting electron-correlation energies along bond-stretching potential energy surfaces. We demonstrate that increasing the body order of the encoding systematically improves prediction accuracy toward the full configuration interaction limit. Remarkably, disconnected many-particle densities constructed from lower-order electronic information achieve accuracy comparable to explicitly correlated two-particle densities in key cases, providing a computationally inexpensive route to higher-body quantum encodings. These findings establish a connection between the many-body structure of classical data and the design of quantum feature maps, offering a systematic, physically interpretable framework for enhancing the expressivity and predictive performance of quantum machine-learning circuits.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40848v1
- Title: Predictive Maintenance at the Edge: An Embedded Anomaly Detection Pipeline Trained with Quantum Optimization
- Authors: Filipe Chagas Ferraz
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.LG
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40848v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40848v1.pdf

Abstract:
This article presents a predictive-maintenance pipeline that detects anomalies in bearings from vibration signals, adapted for deployment at the edge on low-cost microcontrollers, and that uses quantum optimization in two steps of its training. The pipeline consists of a step of vibration feature extraction and a step of classification with a one-class support vector machine (OCSVM). The features are selected by minimum redundancy and maximum relevance (mRMR) with the variational quantum eigensolver (VQE), under a limit on computing time. The OCSVM is adapted for inference at the edge without floating-point operations and is trained by a variational quantum algorithm with Pauli-correlation encoding (PCE). In small-scale tests on a simulator, with the Paderborn bearing dataset, the VQE found the optimal selection in 36% of the runs with Adam, and PCE chose better support vectors than chance, until a classical local search made the two equal. A classical baseline, the usual OCSVM trained by SMO with its weights rounded, matched them without any search.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40852v1
- Title: Colloquium: Quantum information processing with ensembles of identical particles
- Authors: Matteo Piccolini, Marcin Karczewski, Jin-Shi Xu, Chuan-Feng Li, Guang-Can Guo, Giuseppe Compagno, Farzam Nosrati, Rosario Lo Franco
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40852v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40852v1.pdf

Abstract:
Identical particles underpin most quantum technologies, but the role of indistinguishability in these processes extends far beyond the symmetrization of many-particle states. This Colloquium presents a unified operational perspective on how particle identity, indistinguishability, exchange statistics, and many-particle interference can instead be harnessed as resources for quantum information processing. We first clarify the notions of entanglement and indistinguishability for identical particles, emphasizing their dependence on the accessible measurements and information. We then review how spatial overlap, multiparticle interference, and path identity enable the generation, extraction, protection, and distillation of coherence and entanglement. Their applications to quantum metrology and imaging, state discrimination, teleportation, entanglement swapping, and quantum cloning are examined. Finally, we discuss the thermodynamic role of indistinguishability, from the Gibbs paradox to quantum thermal machines and statistics-driven engines. Across these settings, indistinguishability emerges as a controllable, task-dependent resource whose benefits are determined by state preparation, exchange statistics, interference, and measurement constraints.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40886v1
- Title: Clifford synthesis is NP-complete
- Authors: Søren Fuglede Jørgensen
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.CC
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40886v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40886v1.pdf

Abstract:
We show that the problem of synthesizing a quantum circuit for a given Clifford operator using at most a specified amount of resources is NP-complete for several resource models of practical interest.   Specifically, we study synthesis of a given Clifford operator using single-qubit Clifford gates and two-qubit Clifford gates, with full qubit connectivity and no additional ancilla qubits. With single-qubit gates free and CNOT as the only two-qubit gate, we show that deciding whether a given target admits an implementation using at most a specified number of CNOT gates is NP-complete, and we show that the problem remains NP-complete when any two-qubit Clifford gate is allowed and the budget counts all such gates. Moreover, we show that both results continue to hold when SWAP gates may be inserted for free, and when single-qubit gates are also considered part of the budget.   In each case, we build on recent work by Li and Guan and show that there are polynomial-time reductions from Hamiltonian path in grid graphs to these decision problems, using a number of qubits quadratic in the number of grid vertices.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40904v1
- Title: Optical Quantum Memory Processing for Quantum Hashing
- Authors: S. A. Moiseev, M. M. Minnegaliev
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.optics
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40904v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40904v1.pdf

Abstract:
Sender authentication is an important requirement for secure quantum communication networks. We propose programmable photon-echo quantum memory processing of a multi-time-bin photonic qudit for implementing a quantum-hash-based verification protocol. In this approach, using QM, sender (Alice) generates a quantum hash state by transforming a single stored photonic wave packet into the programmable photonic qudit. Bob's memory coherently maps the received time-bin photonic qudit into a single output mode according to phase encoding. Our scheme relies on a time-resolved coherent detection method introduced here, in which the coherent responses from individual time-bins are sequentially accumulated within Bob's spin coherence prior to the final sender verification. The key advantage of this coherent detection for quantum hashing is shown, and its potential applications are also discussed. The performed analysis demonstrates that experimentally verified parameters of contemporary optical quantum memories enable a proof of principle realization of the proposed architecture. These results reveal an additional role for quantum memories, serving not only as synchronization and repeater resources for long-distance quantum communication, but also as active elements for quantum hashing in conventional quantum key distribution systems.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40941v1
- Title: Hypergraph cycle enumeration for efficient inference of planted k-XOR with smooth trade-off
- Authors: Arman Babakhani, Enrico Fontana, Junhyung Lyle Kim, Shouvanik Chakrabarti, Ruslan Shaydulin
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.DS; stat.CO
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40941v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40941v1.pdf

Abstract:
We introduce hypergraph cycle enumeration (HCE), a simple spectral framework for detecting and recovering planted signals in noisy hypergraph inference problems. We study HCE on one representative problem from this class, namely planted noisy k-XOR, also known as sparse Learning Parity with Noise (LPN). Both theoretically and empirically, HCE achieves comparable recovery behavior to Kikuchi method, matching Kikuchi hierarchy. We identify parameter regimes where HCE outperforms Kikuchi method, showing that neither algorithm is universally better. We also show a subquadratic quantum speedup for HCE by replacing one of the algorithm's steps with a quantum walk. Our classical and quantum algorithms do not refute the quartic quantum speedup of quantum Kikuchi method, which remains valid for the practically relevant regime of large size and high noise strength.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40988v1
- Title: Variational Programs with Open Clocks
- Authors: Javaria Ghafoor, Amr Sabry
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40988v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40988v1.pdf

Abstract:
Variational quantum algorithms leave the durations of Hamiltonian evolutions open for a classical optimizer to set. We present VHamilton, a minimal extension of the Hamilton quantum programming language in which such durations are open clocks: one program denotes a family of Hamiltonian trajectories, and a valuation fills its clocks with exact dyadic times, so that each instance is an ordinary Hamilton program that compiles to circuits as before. Because the semantics stays exact and affine in the clocks, the Hamiltonian structure of a variational program remainsavail able for reasoning about every valuation at once. Equivalence becomes a finite calculation, which we mechanize in Agda for a fragment and use to prove the VHamilton form of the compiler rewrite "rzz(t) = cx; rz(t); cx" for every clock value. We then show what the same structure supports in a two-qubit chemistry eigensolver written in VHamilton and run through a Haskell prototype. It supports a fusion of commuting blocks, a closed form and an expressibility proof for the ansatz, and an exact split of the error between the precision of the target and that of the clocks, with a priori bounds. It also supports a coordinate search whose queries per sweep, fixed by the spectra of the generators, do not grow with the precision. On the benchmark this search reaches the numerically verified optimum of a grid of 134 million valuations in 53 energy queries. It finds the grid optimum in only 21 of 30 validation settings, however, so the gain is conditional, not a global-optimization guarantee.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41017v1
- Title: Preparation of a Laughlin-like fractional Chern insulator on a superconducting quantum processor
- Authors: Kamil Dutkiewicz, Bárbara Andrade, Geza Giedke, Tobias Grass
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.mes-hall; cond-mat.quant-gas
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41017v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41017v1.pdf

Abstract:
Fractional Chern insulators (FCIs) are lattice analogues of fractional quantum Hall states, hosting topological order and fractionalized anyonic excitations. Their realization poses a demanding target for quantum simulation. Here, we realize a bosonic FCI on an IBM Nighthawk superconducting quantum processor using classically optimized variational circuits. We target a Laughlin-like state that is an exact zero-energy ground state of a long-range parent Hamiltonian on a torus, eliminating physical edges and providing a favorable geometry for accessing fractional quantum Hall physics in a small system. On a $4\times4$ qubit lattice, we prepare both the four-boson FCI ground state and a three-boson state hosting two quasiholes. Measurements of particle densities and correlations reproduce the characteristic structure of the target states, while reconstructed chiral magnetization currents resolve the circulating current pattern associated with the quasiholes. The preparation of these strongly correlated topological states with shallow circuits establishes a route toward the digital simulation and manipulation of fractionalized excitations, including measurements of topological invariants and anyonic braiding.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41033v1
- Title: Vacuum amplification across a programmable bosonic Bogoliubov stability boundary in a trapped-ion chain
- Authors: Lu Qi, Duanyang Wang, Kenneth R. Brown
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.atom-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41033v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41033v1.pdf

Abstract:
A quantum oscillator always carries vacuum fluctuations, and modulating its parameters converts them into correlated pairs of real quanta. This vacuum amplification underlies squeezed light and Hawking radiation. In a closed quadratic system, pair creation competes with the energy splitting of the modes, and growth turns exponential only once pairing overcomes the splitting. Here we realize this competition in two motional modes of a trapped-ion chain. A two-mode-squeezing drive creates the pairs, and a beam-splitter coupling splits the motion into two coupled modes at different frequencies. The two rates are tuned and calibrated independently. Starting near the motional ground state, we cross the stability boundary in two ways. First, with the drive at the sum of the two bare-mode frequencies, the phonon number stays bounded until the pairing rate exceeds the splitting and grows exponentially beyond it. Second, tuning the drive into resonance with one coupled mode lowers the threshold to zero, and only that mode is amplified into a squeezed vacuum. Separate control of the two interactions makes the threshold for vacuum amplification programmable. This control can be used to simulate particle creation and quantum phase transitions, and to operate parametrically driven bosonic devices close to their stability limit.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41120v1
- Title: Quantum Vehicle Routing Problem: a smart initialization strategy for ill-conditioned optimization landscapes
- Authors: Emanuele Casciaro, Mario De Simone, Stefano Martina, Fabio Mascherpa, Alfonso Amendola, Filippo Caruso
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.ET; math.CO; math.OC
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41120v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41120v1.pdf

Abstract:
With the expansion of complex transport infrastructure, new paths for linking different destinations have emerged, allowing the definition of multiple routes. The introduction of new roads may also lead to disruptions in traffic flow. This problem can be generalized into a special case of VRP, which aims to find the optimal route for a fleet of vehicles within a network of destinations. Finding solutions to this problem requires an exponential number of operations, hindering the ability of exact solvers to deal with large instances, which must therefore be tackled using heuristics, often leading to suboptimal results. Quantum-based algorithms may allow us to find exact solutions in polynomial time with high probability, offering a potential future alternative to classical solvers and in this work, we focus on the QAOA. Our novel contributions are a parameter-initialization strategy that improves the convergence probability and the integration of the subtour constraints directly into the optimization without using additional qubits by employing an approximation of exponential equality constraints. We believe that our results pave the way for effectively solving instances of VRP using quantum-based solutions, leveraging the space-efficient problem encoding and our initialization strategy, which limits the effect of barren-plateau under random parameter initialization.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41121v1
- Title: Entangled Measurement Outcomes for Optimal Postselection in Quantum Batteries
- Authors: Yali Du, Zhipei Zhou, Xiaoli Hu
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41121v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41121v1.pdf

Abstract:
Postselection can enhance the performance of a selected quantum battery branch, raising the question of whether the optimum can always be achieved by product measurements on the auxiliary subsystems. For battery auxiliary states obtained by mixing a pure state with global white noise of strength p, the conditional ergotropy reduces to a generalized Rayleigh quotient. For 0<p<1, product postselection attains the joint optimum if and only if the eigenspace associated with the largest generalized eigenvalue contains a product vector. For two auxiliary qubits, a strict product-joint gap therefore occurs precisely when this eigenspace is one dimensional and spanned by an entangled vector. Noisy W and GHZ states display qualitatively different behavior: the three qubit W state exhibits a strict gap throughout 0<p<1, whereas the three qubit GHZ state admits a product optimum. For interacting two qubit batteries, the optimal postselection can switch from a Bell outcome to a product outcome as the coupling strength is varied. By contrast, the outcome averaged capacity and ergotropy are independent of the measurement basis, showing that the joint measurement advantage is genuinely postselective.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41140v1
- Title: Probing Operator Spreading with Local Quantum Fluctuations
- Authors: Bertúlio de Lima Bernardo
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41140v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41140v1.pdf

Abstract:
Measuring out-of-time-ordered correlators (OTOCs) is experimentally challenging, often requiring effective reversal of many-body dynamics. Here, we propose an alternative approach to probing operator spreading through local quantum fluctuations, using only forward evolution and local measurements. Motivated by the Heisenberg uncertainty relation, we introduce a differential local variance and establish a rigorous bound connecting it to operator noncommutativity. For a ferromagnetic Heisenberg chain in the single-magnon regime, we derive an exact relation between the local variance and a squared-commutator OTOC. In integrable and nonintegrable Ising chains, the differential variance and OTOCs reveal qualitatively consistent propagation fronts. Our results establish local quantum fluctuations as an experimentally accessible, state-dependent, and complementary diagnostic of operator spreading.

- Title: Encoded reversible addition in constant Toffoli depth
- Authors: Aidan Epperly, Isaac H. Kim, Mingyu Lee
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41142v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41142v1.pdf

Abstract:
We show that reversible in-place quantum addition modulo $2^n-1$ can be implemented in constant Toffoli depth using $O(n)$ space, provided that the variables are encoded in a certain non-standard form. Using measurement-based uncomputation, the Toffoli depth can be reduced to one. We also present space-depth tradeoffs and an application to programmable rotations.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41180v1
- Title: Quantum query complexity of Hurwitz stability testing
- Authors: Maxwell Fitzsimmons, Mohamed Serry, Meenu Kumari, Jun Liu
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.CC
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41180v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41180v1.pdf

Abstract:
Determining whether every trajectory of the linear system $\dot{x}=Ax$ converges to zero (or, equivalently, whether $A$ is Hurwitz stable) is a fundamental problem in control theory. It arises, for example, in verifying the stability of feedback controllers and state observers, as well as of linearized dynamical models. We study the quantum query complexity of deciding whether a matrix $A\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}$ is Hurwitz stable, given an exact normalized block-encoding of $A$. The query complexity is characterized in terms of the system dimension $n$ and a supplied lower bound $0<ν\leq ω_A$ on the imaginary-axis distance $ω_A:=\min_{t\in\mathbb{R}}σ_{\min}(A-\mathrm{i}tI)$, where $σ_{\min}(A)$ is the smallest singular value of $A$. We prove a query-complexity lower bound of $Ω(\sqrt{n}/ν)$, which holds even when $A$ is restricted to be real and diagonal. For general matrices with nonzero $ω_A$, we encode an approximation of the right-half-plane spectral projector $\operatorname{sign}^+(A):=(I+\operatorname{sign}(A))/2$ as a block of a matrix inverse, where $A$ is Hurwitz if and only if the projector is zero. To test this, we produce a quantum algorithm which determines if a $(β,b,1/(4\sqrt{n}))$-block-encoded projector matrix (like $\operatorname{sign}^+(A)$) is zero or not; for constant success probability, it has complexity $Θ(β\sqrt{n})$. When the zero test is applied to specific approximations of $\operatorname{sign}^+(A)$, this yields an upper bound of $O(\sqrt{n}ν^{-2}\log(\sqrt{n}/ν))$ queries in the general case, and $O(\sqrt{n}ν^{-1}\log n)$ when $A$ is Hermitian or normal. The latter upper bound matches the lower bound up to a logarithmic factor. Thus the dimension dependence of quantum Hurwitz testing is nearly optimal, while the remaining factor in the non-normal case isolates an open conditioning gap.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41194v1
- Title: Anomalous superradiant phases in a quantum Rabi zigzag chain under a synthetic gauge field
- Authors: Jun-Bo Tang, Lin-Jun Li, Bin Wei, Yu-Yu Zhang
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41194v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41194v1.pdf

Abstract:
We present the phase diagram of a one-dimensional quantum Rabi zigzag chain under an artificial gauge field, which is implemented via phase-modulated next-nearest-neighbor (NNN) hopping. For a system size of $N=8$, we analytically determine the order parameters and critical thresholds, identifying five distinct superradiant phases. For strong nearest-neighbor (NN) coupling, the system exhibits either a ferro-superradiant (FSR) or an antiferro-superradiant (AFSR) phase depending on its sign. Increasing NNN coupling induces a stripe antiferro-superradiant (sAFSR) phase with antiparallel NNN and parallel NN displacements, which is distinct from the conventional AFSR phase. Tuning the magnetic flux drives the system into chiral or antichiral superradiant phases with opposite ground-state photon currents, which are different from the conventional superradiant phases. Remarkably, geometric frustration within each triangular plaquette stabilizes chiral phases even at small magnetic flux while suppressing the sAFSR phase, which vanishes for $N=6$. Mapping the system onto an effective magnetic Hamiltonian yields an analogous phase diagram featuring distinct magnetic phases, offering insight into the anomalous superradiant phases. This equivalence establishes a quantum optical platform for simulating chiral magnetism.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41195v1
- Title: Biorthogonal universal quantum control
- Authors: Chen-yi Zhang, Jun Jing
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41195v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41195v1.pdf

Abstract:
As a paradigm for describing open-quantum-system dynamics, the non-Hermitian Schrödinger equation generally modifies state norms, complicating coherent control over the system's Hilbert space. With a general instantaneous frame spanned by biorthogonal ancillary bases, we develop a biorthogonal quantum-control theory for discrete-variable systems governed by time-dependent non-Hermitian Hamiltonian. In particular, we derive a necessary and sufficient condition for extracting the transitionless pathways that transport parallel in ket and bra states. When this condition is satisfied by a set of projectors constructed with biorthogonal basis states, the non-Hermitian Hamiltonian becomes diagonal in the relevant biorthogonal subspace, enabling arbitrary initial state to evolve as a superposition of controlled transitionless passages. We further identify the parametric constraints under which the transformation linking the initial and final states becomes unitary. As a result, the final system state is automatically normalized even though the transitional dynamics is nonunitary. Our theory is justified by the bidirectional population transfer and a holomonic quantum gate implemented by a non-Hermitian three-level system in $Λ$ type. For the population transfer, controlled gain and loss reduce the required coherent pulse area, enabling shorter transfer times than the Hermitian benchmark with the same average Rabi frequency. Our work integrates the universal quantum control (UQC) into the biorthogonal representation, displaying full-rank passage engineering with coherent operations in non-Hermitian systems.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41198v1
- Title: Quantum Advantage in Beam-Displacement Estimation for Inter-Satellite Quantum-Secure Time Transfer
- Authors: Ziqing Wang, Robert Malaney
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.optics
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41198v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41198v1.pdf

Abstract:
In this work, we investigate the practical feasibility of achieving a quantum advantage, in the context of inter-satellite quantum communication, of satellite position estimation. The investigation focuses on a quantum-enhanced scheme in which squeezing is added to the derivative mode of a Gaussian probe beam as a means of estimating photon-beam displacement. The performance of the scheme is compared against the standard quantum limit achieved by an optimal classical beam-displacement estimation scheme under a fair same-total-energy constraint. We find that although the benefit provided by squeezing rapidly diminishes as the inter-satellite distance increases due to diffraction-induced beam spreading and subsequent truncation by the finite receiver aperture, a quantum advantage can still be expected over inter-satellite distances up to approximately 100 km under practical satellite configurations. This distance scale between satellites is anticipated for Starlink-type satellite constellations.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41268v1
- Title: Testing hardware quantum machine learning claims with a null circuit: fill prediction on Binance futures with 129 qubits of an IBM Heron processor
- Authors: Muhammad Faryad
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41268v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41268v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum processors are now used as feature generators for classical models on tabular data. A circuit encodes each record, the device returns expectation values, and a classical model learns from them. A recent report claimed large gains from such features, gains that appeared only on noisy hardware. These claims are hard to check, because a quantum device is not a static function. Its state changes from job to job and from one calibration to the next, and that drift can leak information about the outcome into the features. We propose a five-step protocol for testing claims of this kind. It runs a circuit that carries no data in the same jobs as the real one. It checks whether the hardware leaves a recognizable mark on each job. It compares random and natural assignments of records to jobs, replays the null results under other submission orders, and closes the leak with a job-purged backtest. We apply it, pre-registered, to the HSBC and IBM study of fill probabilities in bond trading, which reported a large hardware-only gain. We use a public analog task and run the study's circuit on a 129-qubit chain of an IBM Heron processor. Under controlled assignment the hardware features give no gain over classical features, and the device reproduces the ideal features about as well as shot noise allows. Yet every job carries a distinct fingerprint. A circuit with no data at all predicts fills well above chance when records are batched by outcome and the same job sits on both sides of the train/test split. Removing that overlap removes the effect. The protocol costs under an hour of processor time and is designed for any hardware feature or kernel pipeline on tabular data.

- Title: Parametric Enhancement of an Optical Spring in a Dark Signal-Recycling Cavity
- Authors: Kaido Suzuki, Ryo Iden, Ken-ichi Harada, Kentaro Somiya
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.optics
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41270v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41270v1.pdf

Abstract:
Optical springs tailor the response of precision sensors. Parametric amplification of the signal field stiffens the spring, extending the precision to higher frequencies. A Michelson interferometer operated at a dark fringe is the most direct way to separate the signal from the carrier for amplification. We experimentally demonstrate such quantum manipulation of the optical spring by placing an optical parametric amplifier in a cavity at the dark port, showing a significant increase of the optical-spring constant with the parametric gain within a factor of four below the oscillation threshold.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41282v1
- Title: Estimation of Quantum Umlaut Information
- Authors: Beomjoon Kim, Donghwa Ji, Kabgyun Jeong
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.IT; cs.LG
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41282v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41282v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantifying correlations is important for understanding how quantum states and channels support information processing. Quantum umlaut information $U$ connects such correlations to reliability in hypothesis testing and certain communication settings, but its known formulas require classical descriptions of the underlying states. To estimate correlations without these descriptions, we introduce measured quantum umlaut information $U^M$ by replacing Umegaki relative entropy with measured relative entropy. Its variational formulation leads to a quantum generative adversarial framework for estimating $U^M$ from measurement statistics of unknown bipartite states and classical-quantum channels. This framework optimizes reference states and measurements without reconstructing the target states or channel outputs. For classical-quantum channels, it also optimizes the input probabilities. We further analyze the relation between $U^M$ and $U$, deriving a state-dependent gap bound and characterizing equality for full-rank bipartite states. Numerical simulations show trained estimates close on average to independently computed numerical references for $U^M$ across the tested states and channels. These estimates could help assess how well quantum memories preserve correlations and how noise or drift affects input-output dependence in quantum transmitters and state-preparation devices.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41311v1
- Title: Optimality of the Petz-Fuchs Smoothed State
- Authors: Kiarn T. Laverick
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41311v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41311v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum state smoothing is an estimation technique that uses past and future measurement information obtained from a continuous-in-time measurement record to reconstruct the state of an open quantum system. Recently, there has been a new approach to quantum state smoothing based on quantum retrodiction known as the Petz-Fuchs smoothed state. Here I prove that this Petz-Fuchs smoothed state majorizes all other smoothed states that only depend on the filtered state (the operator conditioned on the past measurement record) and the retrofiltered effect (the operator describing the future measurement record). To do this, I characterize the set of all possible smoothed states satisfying this condition.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41317v1
- Title: PyQLAS: A Quantum-Classical Hybrid Solver Library for Linear Systems
- Authors: Meng-Han Dou, Teng-Yang Ma, Chuang-Chao Ye, Zhao-Yun Chen, Guo-Ping Guo
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41317v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41317v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum algorithms solve a linear system at a cost that grows only polylogarithmically in its dimension, but on present-day hardware that advantage is realised only if the quantum and the classical parts of a solve are apportioned deliberately. We present PyQLAS, a quantum--classical hybrid library for linear systems that exposes this apportionment as a design parameter rather than a fixed assumption. PyQLAS builds three nested solvers around a single variational core---a variational solver, an outer residual iteration, and a Krylov-subspace projection---so that the quantum subroutine can be held at a fixed circuit width while the classical host absorbs the growth of the problem. Around them, the library supplies preconditioning at two independent levels, dimension alignment, checkpointing, and device-budget control, and a solve written against the interface runs unchanged on an exact state-vector simulator, on a local sampler, and on a real superconducting processor. The library is validated on the IEEE 118-bus power flow, a standard test system in which the linear solver is embedded in a classical Newton iteration. There, the iteration converges to the specified tolerance and reproduces the classical reference solution to within $2.3\times10^{-8}$ p.u.\ in voltage magnitude and $3.4\times10^{-6}$ degrees in voltage angle.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41341v1
- Title: Time-Domain Multiplexing for Resource-Efficient Heralded GKP State Preparation
- Authors: Ke-Ming Hu, Jun-Jie He, Yu-Ze Zhu, Pei-Lin Du, Lin Yang, Hao Tang, Xian-Min Jin
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41341v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41341v1.pdf

Abstract:
Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill (GKP) states are key resources for fault-tolerant photonic quantum computing, but their conditional preparation via Gaussian boson sampling involves a trade-off between source count and photon-number resolution. Distributing heralding photons across more measured modes reduces the resolution required per mode, yet increases the number of simultaneously operated squeezed sources in fully squeezed spatial architectures. Here, we show that time-domain multiplexing (TDM) can reduce these physical source requirements while preserving GKP state quality. Using an end-to-end differentiable Bargmann framework, we find that TDM circuits reusing one or two squeezed sources achieve fidelities comparable to fully squeezed spatial circuits across several configurations. Selected nine-mode configurations retain fidelities above 0.95 with one photon heralded in each measured mode, mitigating detector resolution demands without a proportional increase in source count. Loss-aware reoptimization further shows that, under the investigated spatial loss models, two-mode TDM maintains fidelities near 0.80 at 1.0 dB per channel, whereas the corresponding spatial benchmarks fall below 0.60. These results demonstrate that temporal reuse provides an effective architectural route for balancing source overhead, detection demands, and state preparation performance in the presence of optical loss.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41388v1
- Title: Loss-aware optimization of realistic GKP gid states
- Authors: Michal Matulík, Petr Marek
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41388v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41388v1.pdf

Abstract:
Realistic preparation of Gottesman-Kitaev-Preskill (GKP) states, the prime candidates for quantum computation with bosonic systems, is inevitably burdened by photon loss. We show how taking this loss into account can lead to more robust quantum states, thereby diminishing its negative influence. Using GKP squeezing as an operational figure of merit, we formulate a loss-aware optimization procedure based on the ground state representation of GKP states. Numerical simulations for square, hexagonal, and asymmetric GKP states show that the optimal preparation generally requires a different initial grid spacing than that of the desired ideal code state. For symmetric and hexagonal lattices, the optimal parameters asymptotically follow the simple scaling relation. The optimized states exhibit better GKP squeezing and greater resilience to losses. These results provide a simple and experimentally relevant strategy for improving realistic photonic GKP state preparation in the presence of known photon loss.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41398v1
- Title: Distilling Knowledge from Search: Decoder Model Factory for Quantum LDPC Codes
- Authors: Anil Bilgin, Wanda Hou, Pranav Srinivas, Yi-Zhuang You
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.dis-nn; cs.AI
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41398v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41398v1.pdf

Abstract:
We introduce GraphMP, a learning-based message-passing decoder for quantum low-density parity-check codes that transfers without fine-tuning across code families, code sizes, physical error rates, and noise models. GraphMP uses local message passing to keep computation scalable as the decoding graph grows. Guided by its predictions, beam search progressively refines candidate error patterns, trading additional decoding time for lower logical error rates. We develop the decoder model factory as a paradigm for training successive models on experience generated by earlier models during decoding. Model-guided search supplies verified trajectories for knowledge distillation, folding computation spent on decoding into reusable training data. Repeated collection and distillation improve the decoder across generations and adapt its strategy as the noise changes. These results motivate a coupled quantum--classical architecture with two interacting cycles: rapid decoding protects quantum information, while slower learning renews the models from operating experience. The long-term vision is a scalable decoder model factory that produces high-performance, low-latency decoders and continually adapts them to evolving quantum hardware.

- Title: Helper-assisted quantum sensing with low dressed-state leakage
- Authors: Shirwan Abdullah
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.mes-hall
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41404v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41404v1.pdf

Abstract:
We study a two-qubit sensing protocol in which only a helper qubit is read out. Calibrated loading and unloading encode a transverse signal in the helper's phase, keep leakage out of a selected dressed-state band small, and return the sensor close to its idle ground state. We derive an information bound for a single sensor whose excitation stays below a prescribed budget, and simulate repeated operation with an uncertain coupling and finite-duration resets in a driven open-system model. At the tested settings, finite measurement records support estimation with infrequent resets. A helper flip at zero coupling, combined with a reversal of the coupling sign, suppresses a static helper offset shared across a record while retaining the signal, at a cost of about 27% of the no-offset information of a plain sequence of equal duration. Under slowly varying noise, averaging each cycle's likelihood over the correct noise law removes most of the added bias, and a wrong noise law can worsen the estimate. A selected directly read sensor with ideal controls and repeated nondemolition verification has the higher information rate. Any practical benefit of avoiding direct readout therefore requires a device-specific disturbance model.

- Title: Real-time BP decoding of a high-rate QEC code
- Authors: Andreas Kell, Daniel Herr, Daniel Bascones García, Alexandru Paler, Davide Orsucci, Francisco Lázaro, Francisco García Herrero
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41413v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41413v1.pdf

Abstract:
We present a real-time FPGA decoder for a high-rate $[[512,174,8]]$ 3D product qLDPC code. We construct a circuit-level detector-error model for a compact syndrome-extraction schedule and propose a small parallel ensemble of row-wise serial min-sum decoders with cumulative memory. The decoder matches $\text{BP+OSD-CS}_\text{10}$ accuracy with one core and Relay-BP accuracy with three cores. The FPGA implementation meets the latency budget of neutral-atom and trapped-ion systems. An ASIC implementation would improve the latency, enabling real time decoding even for superconducting systems. At a physical error rate of $p=10^{-3}$, three FPGA devices decode 174 logical qubits with a logical error rate of $5\times10^{-7}$ and effective distance 6. For $p=10^{-4}$, and assuming the number of BP iterations is capped to 30, the decoder's average latency is $5.1\,$\textmu s and its worst-case latency is $97.9\,$\textmu s.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41421v1
- Title: Analytic level statistics of the quantum Rabi model
- Authors: Zi-Min Li, Long-Jie Li, German Sierra, Murray T. Batchelor
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41421v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41421v1.pdf

Abstract:
We derive an analytic expression for the distribution of level spacings in the quantum Rabi model. At high energies, the spectrum in a fixed parity sector takes the asymptotic form of a uniformly spaced ladder modulated by a decaying oscillation. The alternating sign of this oscillation separates successive spacings into two branches. For a finite window containing many oscillations, counting how often the two branches exceed a given spacing yields an analytic expression for the spacing density at leading order. The oscillation amplitude at the lower end of the window sets the range of spacings, and its value at the upper end fixes the positions of two cusps that form twin peaks. Spacing histograms from exact diagonalization agree with the analytic density, including its spacing range and twin peak positions. At leading order, the same spectral analysis also yields an expression for the ratio of consecutive spacings, a statistic that requires no spectral unfolding. The resulting ratio statistics agree closely with those from exact diagonalization. For windows that continue to contain many oscillations, the spacing and ratio peaks move toward unity at higher energies as the spectrum approaches a uniformly spaced ladder. The same counting approach can be explored in other systems with controlled spectral asymptotics, slowly varying spacing branches, and windows containing many oscillations.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41431v1
- Title: oracq: A Modular Framework for Implementing and Analyzing Quantum Scientific Computing Algorithms
- Authors: Zhao-Yun Chen, Ming-Yang Tan, Guo-Ping Guo
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41431v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41431v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum algorithms for scientific computing are specified against access models such as block-encodings, sparse oracles, state preparations, and quantum random-access memories. Their concrete realization is deliberately left open, yet it is a research variable that changes both the numerical behavior and the resource cost of the program, so every reported conclusion is attributable to one realization. In this paper, we present oracq, a Python framework that makes this choice a managed and checkable decision. Our claim is linguistic rather than infrastructural. Algorithms are stored as persistent register-level programs whose oracle slots are declared and typed but unimplemented. A separate binding pass links compatible realizations, so one stored algorithm can be re-bound to a gate network, a QRAM-backed table, or compiled reversible arithmetic and compared across them. Typed QRAM resources, a compiler from pure Python numerical formulas to reversible fixed-point circuits, and block-encoding algebra with qubitization and QSVT-style kernels supply the constructions. Two end-to-end workflows exercise the framework, a quantum finite-volume pipeline for the compressible Euler equations and a quantum homotopy-analysis pipeline that lifts nonlinear PDEs to composable linear input models. The verification methodology separates implementation error from method error, and logical resource accounts cover programs before and after binding. The framework is openly available at https://github.com/IAI-USTC-Quantum/oracq.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41444v1
- Title: Almost-Quantum Correlations Violate Information Causality
- Authors: Anandamay Das Bhowmik, Manik Banik
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41444v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41444v1.pdf

Abstract:
Information causality (IC) posits that communicating m classical bits cannot give the receiver access to more than m bits of information about the sender's independent data. Although IC holds in quantum theory and excludes a broad class of supra-quantum correlations, its validity for the almost-quantum set has remained open. This semidefinite relaxation of the quantum set satisfies nontrivial communication complexity, no advantage for non-local computation, macroscopic locality, local orthogonality, and numerical evidence has suggested that it may also satisfy IC. Here we show that almost-quantum correlations violate IC, and do so without bound. Unlike earlier violations of IC, which rely on biased guesses, ours is driven by zero-error certificates: each is rare but never wrong, carries information linear in its probability, and vouches for many of the sender's bits at once. This establishes a sharp dichotomy: either IC is a valid physical principle that excludes these correlations, or any nonsignalling theory realizing them admits no mutual information obeying consistency, data processing and the chain rule. In the latter case, we discuss why the chain rule is the natural property to relinquish, and how this possibility relates to histories-based formulations and to locality defined relative to the state rather than presupposed in a fixed spacetime.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41448v1
- Title: An Unbounded Separation of Almost-Quantum and Commuting-Operator Correlations in Zero-Error Communication
- Authors: Rafiuddin Gazi, Biswadeep Chatterjee, Jayashree Karmakar, Ram Krishna Patra, Manik Banik, Anandamay Das Bhowmik
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41448v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41448v1.pdf

Abstract:
Almost-quantum correlations form the $\mathtt{1+AB}$ level of the Navascués--Pironio--Acín hierarchy. They strictly contain the quantum set, are characterized by a single semidefinite program, arise in Sorkin's quantum measure theory, and satisfy several principles proposed to single out quantum theory. We show that they are nevertheless unboundedly stronger than quantum correlations in a zero-error communication task. Alice and Bob receive labels $s,t\in[N]$, $N\ge3$; after one bit from Alice, Bob must decide whether $s=t$, never erring but allowed to abstain. For every model of quantum correlations, from finite-dimensional tensor products to commuting operators, the optimal worst-case probability of a conclusive answer is exactly $1/N$, the value attained with shared randomness, so the bound does not depend on the resolution of Tsirelson's problem. An explicit family of almost-quantum correlations attains $1/19$ for every $N$. For certificates of equality from binary-outcome boxes, we solve the underlying semidefinite programs in closed form. The almost-quantum optimum equals $N^2/(17N^2-80N+64)$ for $N\ge8$ and tends to $1/17$. The first level of the hierarchy, which coincides with the set singled out by macroscopic locality, attains $N/[8(N-2)]$, which tends to $1/8$; its optimal boxes lie outside the almost-quantum set for every $N\ge5$. For longer messages, entanglement saves at most one bit over shared randomness, whereas almost-quantum correlations reduce the one-way zero-error cost of deciding equality of $n$-bit strings from $n$ bits to at most $13$. The quantum bound also yields a linear witness of post-quantumness on a face of the nonsignalling polytope.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41517v1
- Title: eCAS: An Orbital Embedding Method for Quantum Computing
- Authors: Anna Brünisholz, Markus Reiher
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.str-el; physics.chem-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41517v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41517v1.pdf

Abstract:
Future fault-tolerant quantum computers will allow us to calculate electronic structures of atomistic systems. However, these systems will be intrinsically limited in size, requiring embedding theories for many systems because of the unfavorable scaling of the number of terms describing the electron-electron interaction in the second-quantized electronic Hamiltonian. Quantum embedding theories address this challenge by partitioning a quantum system into a region of interest, the impurity, and its (larger) complement, the environment. However, currently available quantum embedding methods place restrictive demands on near-future quantum computers, such as the need to measure reduced density matrices or the need for repeated evaluations. Here, we introduce the new orbital embedding method eCAS that steers clear of these pitfalls by constructing a multi-CAS problem, the active spaces of which are localized and can be solved independently of each other with any quantum algorithm. This also allows for the development of comparatively simple gradient expressions. eCAS leverages the Schmidt decomposition to construct initial impurity and bath orbitals and augments them with localized environment orbitals, based on a mutual correlation criterion. The mutual correlation criterion makes eCAS systematically improvable without the need for any iteratively determined quantities.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41524v1
- Title: High-efficiency photonic GHZ measurements with linear optics
- Authors: Stefan N. van den Hoven, Love A. Pettersson, Anders S. Sørensen, Stefano Paesani
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.optics
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41524v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41524v1.pdf

Abstract:
Probabilistic and destructive Bell-measurements, also known as fusions, constitute a fundamental primitive for photonic quantum technologies. These operations rely only on pairwise photonic interference, whereas the computational power of photonic quantum systems originates from many-body interference. Here, we introduce a family of high-efficiency linear-optical GHZ measurements based on four-mode interferometers and show that incorporating many-body interference into this ubiquitous primitive leads to more efficient entangling operations. For the four-photon case, the success probability is $9/32$, and adaptive variants ensure that unsuccessful events project onto useful stabilizer states. We further show that the approach naturally generalizes to larger GHZ measurements, yielding an exponentially higher success probability than previous linear-optical schemes. Finally, we demonstrate practical advantages for graph-state resource generation, entanglement distribution between remote spin-photon interfaces, and fusion-based quantum computing. Our results establish high-efficiency many-body measurements as a new primitive for scalable photonic quantum information processing.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41528v1
- Title: FaultScope: A software package for fast error attribution in quantum error correction
- Authors: Yuguo Shao, Zhenyu Chen, Ningfeng Wang, Song Cheng, Zhaohui Wei, Zi-Wen Liu, Zhengwei Liu
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41528v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41528v1.pdf

Abstract:
We introduce FaultScope, a classical simulator for quantum error correction circuits, implemented in Rust with a Python interface. FaultScope estimates logical error rates and their derivatives with respect to component error rates from the same sampled and decoded shots, without modifying or differentiating through the decoder. These derivatives enable error attribution to individual noisy components, helping identify noise hotspots for targeted noise reduction. FaultScope supports forward simulation of error propagation through circuits and direct sampling of detector and logical observable outcomes from detector error models. In our noiseless and noisy benchmarks, FaultScope has the lowest runtime among the tested simulators at every size. Computing all derivatives adds only 0.33--0.97\% to the runtime of logical error rate estimation across five repetition code sizes with 7--2047 error locations. FaultScope can be used to benchmark large-scale quantum error correction schemes and guide targeted noise reduction efficiently, thereby facilitating the design and optimization of fault-tolerant quantum computing architectures.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41542v1
- Title: Symmetries and model reduction in a $\cos 2\varphi$ transmon qubit
- Authors: Erwan Roverc'h, Kyrylo Gerashchenko, Alvise Borgognoni, Benoit Douçot, Alexandru Petrescu, Rémi Robin, Zaki Leghtas
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41542v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41542v1.pdf

Abstract:
Engineered symmetries in superconducting circuits can protect quantum information; much like selection rules in atomic physics, they forbid transitions between the qubit states. Protected circuits, however, combine several loops and islands and host multiple nonlinearly coupled modes, calling for reduced low-energy models that inherit the circuit symmetries and make the protection manifest. We develop such a reduction for the $\cos 2\varphi$ transmon qubit, recently realized experimentally with a two-Cooper-pair interferometer, whose circuit hosts three coupled modes. Exploiting the separation of energy scales between the heavy transmon mode and the light internal modes of the interferometer, we derive an effective single-band Born-Oppenheimer model, including its geometric correction, and extend it to several bands. We identify the symmetries of the full and reduced Hamiltonians and derive exact selection rules. Finally, we identify a parameter regime beyond the single-band description that suppresses flux-noise dephasing without degrading charge-noise protection.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41608v1
- Title: Quantum Bernstein--Kolmogorov--Arnold approximation
- Authors: Sviatoslav V. Dzhenzher
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; math.FA
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41608v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41608v1.pdf

Abstract:
This paper addresses the expressivity and representation gap in Quantum Neural Networks by establishing a quantum analogue of the classical Kolmogorov--Arnold representation theorem.   We propose a constructive approximation framework over fixed input coherent states, where the dependence of the function argument is embedded entirely in coordinate qubits, while the target function dictates a single outer unitary evolution.   By applying a Sprecher-type representation to the rotation angle of the state, we demonstrate that the multidimensional operator factorises into \(2d+1\) commuting universal inner observables.   We prove that the resulting measurement probability precisely reproduces the multidimensional Bernstein polynomial of the target function.   For any continuous multivariate function, the joint quantum state converges uniformly to the target output register, linking classical superposition theory with probabilistic and coherent quantum circuits.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41626v1
- Title: Windowed Catodyne Measurements: Continuum Approximation versus Exact Discrete Photon-Number Treatment
- Authors: Elahe Nahvifard, Mohammad Reza Bazrafkan
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41626v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41626v1.pdf

Abstract:
We analyze a family of measurements obtained by retaining only a finite window of the photon-sum variable in catodyne detection, where a Schrödinger cat state serves as the local oscillator. In the continuum approximation, the window half-width appears to act as a continuous knob for coherence. However, a treatment on the discrete photon-number lattice shows that, in the strong-LO regime, the coherence conditioned on the difference outcome is not suppressed by the window. The exact parity-sensitive success probability is a staircase function of and , whose plateau structure and magnitude the continuum formulas fail to capture, especially for small and narrow windows. For remote preparation of cat states, an exact calculation with a finitely squeezed resource shows that the remote state keeps a purity close to one when x is resolved, and loses purity mainly when x is not recorded. Our results clarify where the continuum approximation of catodyne detection can be trusted and where the discrete photon-number structure must be used.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41649v1
- Title: Quantum machine learning geospatial time-series by seasonal encoding
- Authors: Devjyoti Tripathy, Reece Robertson, Josey Stevens, Catherine Nakalembe, Sebastian Deffner
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41649v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41649v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum data reuploading models can increase the expressive capacity of variational quantum models, but do not necessarily improve predictive performance. In the present work, we investigate this behavior for seasonal time series forecasting and show that data encoding determines how well temporal structures are represented as circuit depth increases. We develop a fidelity-based quantum geometric description in which repeated encodings contribute to an effective input generator, so that reuploading depth changes the quantum geometry through which approximately recurrent inputs are distinguished. We construct fixed and adaptive seasonal encodings that represent observation magnitude and seasonal phase in distinct Bloch-sphere coordinates. We evaluate these encodings for monthly precipitation derived from ERA5-Land, a global reanalysis dataset, from a representative semi-arid, drought-prone tropical region in East Africa, using a 14-month lag window and reuploading depths $L$= 1-6. We find that in ideal simulations, standard value-based encodings degrade markedly with depth, whereas adaptive seasonal encodings preserve temporal correlation, spectral structure, and forecasting accuracy. Noisy simulations show that this encoding hierarchy persists at shallow and intermediate depths, while larger depths are limited by circuit noise and optimization difficulty. The seasonal quantum models remain competitive with established classical forecasting methods (ARIMA, random forests, and LSTMs). These results identify structure-aware quantum data encoding as a general strategy for building deeper quantum reuploading models.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41653v1
- Title: Simulating two-Dimensional decaying vortex structure with the Carleman-Lattice-Boltzmann method
- Authors: Luca Cappelli, Massimo Berneschi, Sauro Succi
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41653v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41653v1.pdf

Abstract:
We perform a numerical study of the free decay of two-dimensional nonlinear vortex structures using the second-order-truncated Carleman-Lattice Boltzmann method (CLB2). To this purpose, we simulate the nonlinear vortex system on a $128\times128$ grid at two initial Reynolds numbers, $Re\approx15$ and $Re\approx46$, by resorting to a highly-tuned multi-GPU implementation of the CLB2 code.   At $Re \approx 15$, CLB2 shows significant accuracy all along the decay process, up to six diffusive times (corresponding to $950$ time steps), with structure-preserving errors below 10\% in amplitude, where by structure-preserving we imply that such errors can be almost entirely reabsorbed by a global time-dependent rescaling factor. At $Re \approx 46$, the evolution is carried out over $2850$ time steps, also corresponding to six diffusive times. In this scenario, however, the errors grow in time well above 10\% in amplitude with a still relatively modest but appreciable effect on the spatial structure of the flow.   These results indicate that CLB2 is capable of preserving the nonlinear evolution of coherent structures over sufficiently long time spans to reach an effective zero steady-state. However CLB2 shows a constant sub-dissipative trend (more energy than the corresponding Lattice Boltzmann configurations) with a deteriorating accuracy at increasing time and Reynolds number.   The present results shed hope that further developments, such as suitable closures instead of truncation and/or various forms of regularization, may lead to a significant improvement of the CLB2 accuracy, prior or even without escalating to expensive higher-order truncations.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41678v1
- Title: Solving the MAX-$k$-CUT problem for hypergraphs with quantum computing
- Authors: V. S. Yarmus, Kh. P. Gnatenko
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41678v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41678v1.pdf

Abstract:
We investigate the MAX-$k$-CUT problem on hypergraphs by considering their representation as spin systems with Ising interactions. The corresponding cost Hamiltonian for MAX-$k$-CUT problem on hypergraphs is reduced to an Ising Hamiltonian with pairwise interactions. This reduction simplifies the solution of the MAX-$k$-CUT problem using quantum computing. As illustrative examples, a 3-uniform tight-star hypergraph and a sunflower hypergraph are considered. The corresponding Hamiltonians are constructed, and quantum protocols for the MAX-$k$-CUT problems are presented. The MAX-3-CUT and MAX-4-CUT instances are investigated for these hypergraphs by varying the number of Quantum Approximate Optimization Algorithm (QAOA) layers from (p=2) to (p=15) with quantum computing on IBM's Qiskit AerSimulator.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41694v1
- Title: Exponential quantum-classical query separations for cyclic Forrelation
- Authors: Yuxin Zhang, Changpeng Shao
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.CC
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41694v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41694v1.pdf

Abstract:
We study the randomized classical query complexity of cyclic Forrelation, a cyclic analogue of $k$-Forrelation [AA15, STOC'15; BS21, STOC'21]. Given $k$ Boolean functions $f_1,\ldots,f_k:\mathbb{F}_2^n\to\{-1,1\}$, the goal is to estimate $T_k=N^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}(D_{f_1}H^{\otimes n}\cdots D_{f_k}H^{\otimes n}),$ where $D_{f_i}=\operatorname{diag}(f_i(x):x\in\mathbb{F}_2^n)$, $H$ is the Hadamard gate, and $N=2^n$. This normalized-trace problem can be solved with $k$ quantum queries in the DQC1 model. Its classical query complexity was left open in [JLS+26, FOCS'26], which conjectured a lower bound of $\widetildeΩ(N^{1-2/k})$.   We establish a nearly complete characterization of the nonadaptive classical query complexity of cyclic Forrelation. For even $k\ge 4$, we prove a tight bound of $Θ_k(N^{1-2/k})$ for constant additive error. For odd $k\ge 5$, we obtain a lower bound of $Ω_k(N^{1-2/(k-1)})$ and an upper bound of $O_k(N^{1-2/k})$. Surprisingly, $k=3$ admits a polylogarithmic-query nonadaptive classical algorithm, disproving the conjecture in this case. These results suggest an unexpected parity-dependent structure: the nonadaptive complexity may be governed by the independence number of the $k$-cycle, leading to the exponent $1-1/\lfloor k/2\rfloor$, rather than the previously conjectured $1-2/k$, which was motivated by a Fourier-growth heuristic. Finally, allowing adaptivity, we prove a lower bound of $Ω(N^{1/4})$ for every $k\ge4$.   Although formally close to $k$-Forrelation, the cyclic structure leads to rather different classical behavior and requires different techniques. Our lower bounds are based on hidden-shift collision constructions, and our upper bounds follow from randomized sampling together with cycle-specific conditional-moment estimates. Our results provide a systematic understanding of its query advantages over classical computation.
