- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41708v1
- Title: Extending Sample-based Quantum Diagonalization to Systems of Linear Equations
- Authors: Taha Selim
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; math.NA
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41708v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41708v1.pdf

Abstract:
Solving systems of linear equations, $Ax=b$, is a ubiquitous task in computational science. The Harrow-Hassidim-Lloyd algorithm requires phase estimation, controlled rotations, and post-selection, impractical on noisy intermediate-scale quantum hardware. We extend sample-based quantum diagonalization (SQD), developed for quantum chemistry, to linear systems. Following the sample-based Krylov variant, the Hermitian matrix $A$ is decomposed in the Pauli basis, the Trotterized time evolution of $|b\rangle$ is sampled at several times to select computational-basis configurations, and the system is solved classically by a Galerkin projection onto the selected subspace. We prove that the subspace solution is exact when the sampled configurations contain the Krylov support of $b$ and optimal in the energy norm for symmetric positive-definite matrices, so that noise-induced configurations cannot degrade it as long as they do not displace the ideal ones. On IBM Quantum hardware, the sampled distribution of a $4\times4$ example deviates from the exact one by eleven times the shot-noise level, yet the solution is recovered exactly. For a localized problem of dimension $N=1024$, SQD selects 33 configurations, reaches a relative error of $10^{-5}$, and its truncated subspaces coincide with the optimal choice; readout errors enlarge the subspace without degrading the accuracy. The tests also show the limitations: the selected subspace coincides with that of inexpensive classical selection, banded matrices require exponentially many Pauli terms in the binary encoding, and Trotter errors populate spurious configurations unless the circuits are deep. An MIT-licensed Python package and notebooks that reproduce the numerical results are provided.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41709v1
- Title: A Highly Nondegenerate Photon-Pair Source for Detector-Matched Quantum Key Distribution
- Authors: Sander van Haagen, Bora Ulu, Alper Ozulker, Wardah Mahmood, Michal Krelina, Utku Tefek, Kadir Durak
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41709v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41709v1.pdf

Abstract:
In a commercial entanglement-distribution system over telecom fibre, the single-photon detectors are the largest part of the hardware cost. In entanglement-based quantum key distribution (QKD), both photons of each pair must be detected. With two telecom photons, each arm needs either an InGaAs single-photon avalanche diode (SPAD) of low efficiency or a superconducting nanowire single-photon detector (SNSPD) with its own cryostat. We study a highly nondegenerate photon-pair source that needs a telecom detector on one arm only. Two periodically poled KTiOPO$_4$ (ppKTP) crystals pumped at 405~nm emit pairs at 548 and 1550~nm. A Si-SPAD with 82\% efficiency detects the 548~nm photon at the source node, and only the 1550~nm photon travels through fibre. A first, unoptimised module in a polarisation-correlated configuration generates about $1.5\times10^6$ pairs/s per mW of pump power. It collects 13\% of the pairs on the 548~nm arm and 23\% on the 1550~nm arm. In an asymptotic BBM92 model, we compare its links with generic $2\times1550$~nm links that use a bright published source at the same pump power. At 50~km, our link with one SNSPD costs about 25\% more per secret bit than the generic link with one SNSPD. With optimistic target values of 52\% collection and twice the brightness, it costs about 40\% as much as the generic link with two SNSPDs. Our link without a cryostat then costs about 70\% less than the generic link with one SNSPD. If every link runs at its own best pump power, the two-SNSPD link is again the cheapest. The source can also serve clock synchronisation and detector calibration, which need only time-correlated pairs. All key rates assume the entangled configuration of the source, which is under development.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41712v1
- Title: Device-independent conference key agreement from two-body correlators with balanced Dicke states
- Authors: Makoto Ishihara, Masahiro Takeoka
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41712v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41712v1.pdf

Abstract:
Device-independent conference key agreement (DI-CKA) enables $N > 2$ distant parties to establish a common secret key without detailed characterization of the source or measurement devices. Many existing protocols rely on full-body Bell correlators, whose measurement can be demanding for a large number of parties. Here, we show that two-body correlators alone suffice for DI-CKA using balanced Dicke states for every even $N \geq 4$. We construct two-body Bell inequalities and derive a tight analytical lower bound on the conditional von Neumann entropy by comparing their Bell operators with those of asymmetric Clauser-Horne-Shimony-Holt inequalities. In the ideal setting, certified key rates can approach $1-h[(N-2)/(4(N-1))]$, where $h$ is the binary entropy function. This rate remains bounded away from zero as $N \rightarrow \infty$, tending to $1-h(1/4) \approx 0.189$ bits per key-generation round. We show that the honest implementation of our protocol is invariant under collective phase noise. Moreover, a complementary full-body Bell construction certifies positive conference-key rates from correlations arbitrarily close to the local set.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41721v1
- Title: Leveraging Quantum Measurement Randomness for Shot-Efficient Diffusion Models
- Authors: Tianhao Guo, Dianxin Zheng, Yuhua Qian, Yaohui Zheng, Yuzhi Yang, Merouane Debbah, Chau Yuen
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.LG
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41721v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41721v1.pdf

Abstract:
In quantum computing, the readout always carries finite-shot sampling noise. Suppressing this noise through repeated measurements raises the cost of a quantum neural network (QNN). Meanwhile, a stochastic diffusion sampler deliberately adds randomness at each reverse (generation) step. This paper turns the measurement noise from a liability into a resource. Specifically, we use the noise quantum measurement produces anyway to replace part of the noise the sampler would otherwise inject. We characterize the measurement error as multinomial sampling noise propagated through a trainable linear readout. To connect the circuit's Born probabilities to the denoising task, we further formulate the diffusion process on a bounded zero-sum hyperplane mapped to the probability simplex. Therefore, we can employ a compensation noise to match the mean and covariance of the shot-noise-free linear update under some minimum shot conditions. Solving this condition for the shot count gives a state-dependent adaptive budget. For the denoising diffusion implicit model (DDIM), this budget increases as the sampler becomes more deterministic. On an eight-dimensional Gaussian mixture, the schedule achieves comparable generation quality with $303$ shots per sample against a high-shot reference of $1.6\times10^{6}$, about $5300\times$ fewer. Latent diffusion experiments on MNIST further validate the proposed mechanism for generation in a learned latent space.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41761v1
- Title: Scaling laws of entanglement entropy in fractal dimensions
- Authors: Zheyu Cheng, Kailin Song, Jian-Wei Liu, Hanxi Sun, Hongyu Zou, Yuanjin Zheng, Yihao Yang, Baile Zhang
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41761v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41761v1.pdf

Abstract:
Entanglement entropy scaling links geometry to quantum correlations and diagnoses quantum criticality. Fractal lattices extend this link to noninteger dimensions, where boundary and bulk dimensions are independent. It remains open which dimension governs entropy scaling and how strongly gap closing can enhance it. Nonlocal occupied-state projectors and the lack of translation symmetry obstruct standard methods. We overcome these obstacles and classify free-fermion entropy scaling with bounds set by the hopping across the subsystem boundary: an upper bound from its rank and the spectral gap, and a lower bound from how much it lowers the ground-state energy. With only negative-energy states filled and this energy lowering proportional to the boundary, the entanglement entropy obeys the boundary law $S_X=Θ\left(L_X^{d_{\mathrm b}}\right)$ under a uniform gap and satisfies $L_X^{d_{\mathrm b}}\lesssim S_X\lesssim L_X^{d_{\mathrm b}}\ln L_X$ when the gap closes no faster than algebraically. Here $L_X$ is the subsystem linear size and $d_{\mathrm b}$ the boundary dimension. Half occupation of the chiral zero modes gives a mixed-state entropy $S_X^{\mathrm{mix}}$ with $\max\left\{S_X,z_X\ln2\right\}\leqslant S_X^{\mathrm{mix}}\leqslant S_X+z_X\ln2$, where $z_X$ is the local zero-mode weight; an extensive $z_X$ thus makes $S_X^{\mathrm{mix}}$ follow the bulk dimension.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41782v1
- Title: Chiral Torsional Double Wells: Tunneling and Polarizability
- Authors: Shah Ishmam Mohtashim, Manas Sajjan, Sabre Kais
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41782v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41782v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum tunneling between mirror-related wells of a torsional potential sets the timescale for coherent interconversion of chiral conformers. We ask whether slower tunneling also weakens the transition polarizability in hydrogen peroxide, hydrogen disulfide, and hydrogen diselenide. We use relaxed CCSD(T)//MP2 potentials and a kinetic model that removes overall molecular rotation to obtain the torsional states, then use static polarizabilities to calculate transition matrix elements between them. In the molecular frame used here, symmetry makes the matrix element of the even dipole component vanish between opposite-parity torsional states, while the odd polarizability component has a nonzero matrix element. The two quantities probe different parts of the torsional landscape: tunneling depends on penetration through the barriers, while the polarizability matrix element forms mainly within the wells. Consequently, tunneling slows sharply across the molecular series even as the matrix element grows. Higher torsional doublets and deuterium substitution further show that large changes in tunneling time can occur with little change in the transition polarizability matrix element. Calculated torsional energies for H$_2$O$_2$ and D$_2$O$_2$ agree with experimental values. Thus, slowing coherent interconversion need not weaken the intrinsic transition polarizability.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41808v1
- Title: Bounding Fidelity of Non-Stabilizer States from Local Pauli Measurements
- Authors: Yi Li, Otfried Gühne, Qiongyi He
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41808v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41808v1.pdf

Abstract:
Nonstabilizerness, commonly referred to as magic, is essential for universal quantum computation. Yet, for pure $n$-qubit states this property is directly linked to the hardness of direct fidelity estimation. We propose a scheme to bound the fidelity on non-stabilizer states by choosing operators with low measurement overhead and using parallel local Pauli measurements to estimate compatible observables. We selectively measure operators that share a permutational symmetry without imposing any assumption on the physical system. This selection reduces the optimization to a linear program with only $O(n)$ variables for bounding fidelity. Our methods can be extended to bound nonlinear quantities like the purity via convex quadratic programs. For hypergraph states that can exhibit extensive nonstabilizerness, we demonstrate for up to $n=500$ qubits that our framework substantially reduces the measurement overhead under both local and global depolarizing noise. These results show that symmetry induced by measurement selection reduces both measurement and computational costs, enabling scalable characterization of magic resources in quantum computing and quantum networks.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41824v1
- Title: Overcoming the Representational Limitations of Amplitude Encoding for Quantum Neural Networks
- Authors: Vincent Gurgul, David Prinz
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.ET; cs.LG; math.OC
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41824v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41824v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum Neural Networks (QNNs) offer a framework for learning functions from classical data, but their expressive power depends critically on how inputs are encoded into quantum states. Angle encoding can require resources that scale with the number of features, whereas amplitude encoding represents $d$ features using only $\mathcal{O}(\log_2 d)$ qubits. This compactness comes at a representational cost: normalization makes conventional amplitude encoding invariant to nonzero real rescaling of the input, up to an unobservable global phase. We show that this invariance induces an exact, architecture-independent restriction on the resulting hypothesis class. No downstream quantum circuit can distinguish inputs differing by nonzero real scaling. We develop a general condition under which deterministic preprocessing can remove this ambiguity and show that nonlinearity alone is insufficient. Based on this analysis, we introduce a fixed nonlinear feature map that provably distinguishes all real inputs under subsequent amplitude encoding, without adding trainable parameters and while retaining logarithmic qubit scaling. We evaluate the resulting QNN on three canonical classification benchmarks and a nonlinear synthetic dataset against conventional amplitude and angle encoding, as well as a classical neural network. Across these tasks, the proposed encoding consistently improves classification accuracy over both quantum encoding baselines by 21$-$44 %. Our results establish normalization-induced scale invariance as a concrete representational bottleneck of amplitude encoding and demonstrate that it can be removed while preserving a compact quantum representation, marking a step toward quantum models capable of capturing arbitrary relationships in classical data.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41831v1
- Title: Toward Quantum Monte Carlo for Sovereign Debt Risk: A proof of concept
- Authors: Jean-Sébastien Dessureault, Alitiana Mijoro Barisoa
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41831v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41831v1.pdf

Abstract:
We present a proof-of-concept pipeline that applies quantum amplitude estimation to the probability that a sovereign debt-to-GDP ratio exceeds a critical threshold at a given horizon. The pipeline combines a reduced two-factor Gaussian debt model linearized around its mean, quantum state preparation on a 32 x 32 grid, a weighted-adder circuit, an integer comparator, and Maximum Likelihood Amplitude Estimation (MLAE). On a noiseless statevector simulator with 500 shots per Grover power, the circuit estimate (a = 0.1152) agrees with the circuit's exact target amplitude (0.1153), and the observed estimation error is consistent with the amplitude-estimation scaling in the number of oracle calls. The model used here is deliberately simple: it admits a closed-form solution, so the contribution is a demonstration of the encoding and estimation workflow, not a practical advantage over classical methods. We discuss the conditions under which such a pipeline could become relevant and the obstacles that remain.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41848v1
- Title: Engineered Learning Problems on Noisy Quantum Processors
- Authors: Esteban Payares, Nadia Milazzo, Ghada Ben Slama, Thomas Cope
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41848v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41848v1.pdf

Abstract:
Despite quantum machine learning being an active topic of research, demonstrations of algorithms run on quantum hardware beating classical methods remain rare. In this work, we demonstrate a significant performance gap using a projected quantum kernel for $n=15,30$ and $45$ qubits over classical methods on an engineered classification problem, and show reproducibility of the results over different quantum processor units. We also demonstrate how error mitigation can widen this gap, both by improving hardware performance but also by increasing the classical difficulty, with different techniques contributing unequally to these phenomena. This work provides strong evidence that superior empirical performance of quantum hardware over all classical techniques is an achievable goal in quantum machine learning, albeit for an artificial problem. However, as with any application relying on observables, beating state-of-the-art simulation methods remains the primary challenge, as our matrix product state results here show.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41874v1
- Title: Hybrid Quantum Algorithms for Trade Surveillance Analytics
- Authors: Bimal Mehta, Daniel musgrove, Devanshu Shekhar, Shiplu Sarkar, Vanshaj Kerni, Varun Puram, Nitesh Kansara, Abinash Pati, et al.
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41874v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41874v1.pdf

Abstract:
Anti-money laundering (AML) and trade surveillance remain largely manual: rule-based monitoring raises alerts of which, by estimate, over 95% are false positives, each reviewed by a human analyst. We benchmark four quantum and hybrid classifiers against classical baselines on a synthetic pump-and-dump detection task: a fidelity-based quantum k-nearest-neighbours classifier (QKNN) with clustering-based routing; a quantum support vector machine (QSVM) on a one-body projected quantum kernel; a 20-qubit quantum neural network (QNN) with a Pauli-ZZ feature map and RealAmplitudes ansatz; and a hybrid quantum neural network (HQNN) in which a small classical network generates every rotation angle of a five-qubit circuit. Baselines are logistic regression, XGBoost, one- and three-hidden-layer neural networks, and classical k-nearest neighbours. All models share one stratified split of 1000 accounts (800 train, 200 test, 20% suspicious) and two 20-feature representations (ANOVA and PCA); QKNN and QSVM are also tested under simulated noise, and the HQNN is run on the ibm_fez processor. The best model is classical XGBoost on ANOVA features, at a suspicious-class F1 of 61.4%. The HQNN is the strongest quantum-hybrid model, at 54.5-57.9% F1 on an ideal simulator and 55.4-59.8% on ibm_fez, ahead of six of eight classical configurations; at a 15% false-positive rate, half its alerts are genuinely suspicious. Versus the QNN (42.0% F1), it raises under half the false alarms at similar recall with a quarter of the qubits. The QKNN reaches 43.5% F1 (44.4% under noise), above classical KNN (35.6%); the QSVM does not exceed 41%. We observe no quantum advantage, but a five-qubit hybrid on today's noisy hardware already matches well-tuned classical models, with room to grow via richer circuits and larger devices. We contribute a like-for-like comparison with classical models on shared data, splits and features.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41890v1
- Title: Control-Noise Trade-offs in Industrially Integrated ${}^{28}\mathrm{Si}/\mathrm{SiGe}$ Spin-1/2 Qubits
- Authors: Leon C. Camenzind, Ik Kyeong Jin, Yi-Hsien Wu, Akito Noiri, Kenta Takeda, Takashi Nakajima, Takashi Kobayashi, Giordano Scappucci, et al.
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.mes-hall
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41890v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41890v1.pdf

Abstract:
Scaling silicon spin qubits to fault-tolerant processors requires semiconductor foundry integration, yet it remains unclear how industrial fabrication and dense device integration affect qubit control and noise. Here we study ${}^{28}\mathrm{Si}/\mathrm{SiGe}$ electron spin-1/2 qubits fabricated using Intel's industry-compatible 300 mm process and incorporating micromagnets for electrically driven spin resonance, using a high-performance device fabricated in the RIKEN research cleanroom as a reference. Primitive $π/2$-gate fidelities reach the 99.99% level in the Intel devices, which also exhibit longer idle coherence, weaker low-frequency correlations and reduced qubit-to-qubit variability across several measured characteristics. In the present Intel devices, the micromagnets are integrated inline with the back-end-of-line (BEOL) contact routing, with their separation from the quantum well set through the vertical stack design. Relative to the reference, their larger separation contributes to weaker longitudinal and transverse magnetic-field gradients. The weaker longitudinal gradient suppresses the conversion of charge noise into qubit-frequency fluctuations, whereas the weaker transverse gradient reduces electrically driven spin-resonance efficiency and makes drive-dependent errors relevant at lower Rabi frequencies. Additional BEOL routing and metal levels required for two-dimensional arrays make such placement decisions increasingly consequential when scaling. These results establish an integration-level control-noise trade-off linking micromagnet placement within densely routed layouts to achievable gate performance, while very high-fidelity operation remains possible in industrially fabricated devices.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41926v1
- Title: Lifting the mixed to the pure: quantum phases of matter from canonical purification
- Authors: Xiaoyang Huang, Shengqi Sang, Chong Wang, Yijian Zou
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech; cond-mat.str-el
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41926v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41926v1.pdf

Abstract:
Pure-state quantum phases are defined through finite-depth local unitary connections. This concept of phases has recently been generalized to mixed states in terms of locally reversible channels. In this work, we establish a fundamental relation between mixed-state phases and pure-state phases. We show that a locally reversible channel between mixed states can be lifted to a local isometry between their canonical purifications; thus, if two mixed states are in the same phase, then their canonical purifications are in the same pure-state phase. Furthermore, an explicit construction of the lifting isometry is given by ``half'' of the Petz recovery map, suggesting that the Petz recovery map \emph{without rotation} is enough to connect mixed state phases. We illustrate our results with two examples: toric code under bit flip noise and Chern insulator under fermion number noise.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41944v1
- Title: Locality, Probability and the Relevance of History
- Authors: Sebastian Horvat
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.hist-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41944v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41944v1.pdf

Abstract:
Relativistic theory and experiments teach us that causal influences are constrained by the speed of light. Deterministic theories incorporate this lesson in $\textit{functional}$ terms: whether an event occurs or not is functionally determined by events that occur in its immediate causal past. In this paper we suggest a natural generalization of this constraint that applies to probabilistic theories: the $\textit{probability}$ that an event occurs is to be functionally determined by the $\textit{probability}$ of the event-occurrences in its immediate causal past. This constraint differs from Bell's criterion of local causality and turns out to imply it only under an additional assumption that requires history to be, in a sense, $\textit{ir}$relevant. Bell-inequality-violating quantum phenomena are thus arguably $\textit{not}$ in tension with relativity, locality, "statistical independence", nor with a supposed "realism". What they instead show is that history is, in a sense, $\textit{relevant}$.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41957v1
- Title: GENIE: learned quantum circuits as embedding impurity solvers, and why compactness, not correlation strength, decides when they work
- Authors: Awadhoot Vijay Loharkar, Arcesio Castañeda Medina
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41957v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41957v1.pdf

Abstract:
Sample-based quantum diagonalization has been used as an impurity solver in density matrix embedding theory (DMET), but always with fixed-form circuits and without comparison to classical selection at the same subspace size. We introduce GENIE, a DMET impurity solver in which a generative transformer learns the sampling circuit, trained on the quantum-selected configuration interaction energy. On N2, hydrogen chains, water and a hydrogen ring, we compare GENIE at equal determinant budget with classical selected configuration interaction (CIPSI, ASCI, heat-bath CI) and with DMET combined with sample-based quantum diagonalization (SQD). GENIE selects: for N2 at 200 determinants it is within a factor of 1.7 of CIPSI and 25 to 1000 times more accurate than the determinants a fixed circuit actually samples, while standard SQD reaches good energies mainly by filling the subspace. GENIE does not beat the best classical selection, and its circuits require fault-tolerant hardware. Its failures are governed by the compactness of the wavefunction relative to the budget, not by correlation strength. A cheap estimate from coupled-cluster amplitudes predicts this compactness. A router built on it never recommended GENIE where it then failed, apart from seed-to-seed variance, and it transferred unchanged to a blind test on new systems. We also identify two benchmarking pitfalls that can create apparent quantum advantages of one to two orders of magnitude.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41964v1
- Title: No generalised contextuality from the vacuum
- Authors: Ruben Campos Delgado, Selman Ipek
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; gr-qc
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41964v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41964v1.pdf

Abstract:
We show that, under certain conditions, generalised contextuality, in the sense introduced by Spekkens, cannot be created from the vacuum of a quantum field. Initial noncontextual qudit states do not become contextual just by interacting with a quantum field. This is verified in two scenarios. First, in a perturbative approach where the time evolution operator is expanded in powers of the coupling constant. Second, in the limit of zero energy gap in the spectrum of the free detector Hamiltonian. Our results are at the opposite side of what has been recently obtained in the literature regarding the harvesting of Kochen-Specker contextuality. Our results demonstrate that the existing notions of contextuality can be independent of one another and that contextuality harvesting depends on which precise notion is used.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41986v1
- Title: Resource efficient Sample Based Quantum Simulation through Fragmentation and Virtual Partitioning of QPU
- Authors: R. Pradeep, Anurag Chaudhary, Ashish Kumar Patra, Anurag K. S. V., Ruchika Bhat, Sai Shankar P., G. Jaiganesh
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41986v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41986v1.pdf

Abstract:
Present-day sample-based quantum algorithms within quantum-centric supercomputing workflows require substantial classical post-processing, large circuit depths to produce chemically relevant samples, and long QPU times to execute the many circuits needed for molecules of chemically relevant size. We introduce FMO-QVM-HCI-HSQD, a resource-efficient sample-based framework that breaks a large problem into fragments, runs the fragments in parallel on virtual regions of the QPU, and reduces circuit depth through a half-qubit ansatz whose samples seed an HCI expansion. We demonstrate the algorithm on ibm_marrakesh and ibm_boston for the water trimer and the carmofur_Mpro complex with up to 52 qubits, benchmarking against FMO-CCSD(T), FMO-CASCI and FMO-HCI. For the water trimer (14 spin orbitals for monomers, 28 for dimers), our method reduces the two-qubit depth by 39% compared to FMO-QVM-SQD and the QPU time by 82% compared to sequential FMO-SQD, and accesses 3% of the determinant subspace versus 40% for FMO-SQD, while achieving more accurate energies. For the carmofur_Mpro complex (16 and 32 spin orbitals), it reduces the two-qubit depth by 47% and the QPU time by 20% compared to FMO-QVM-SQD, and accesses 0.007% of the determinant subspace versus 40%, while remaining within chemical accuracy. Extending the carmofur_Mpro active space in the 6-31G basis to 52 and 104 spin orbitals, sampled on 26 and 52 qubits, the total energy deviates from FMO-HCI by only 1.22 mHa. These results show that combining fragmentation, virtual QPU partitioning and half-qubit sampling makes sample-based quantum simulation of chemically relevant systems feasible on current hardware.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33

- Title: Logical Bell Inequalities for Marginal Wigner Negativity
- Authors: Sanchit Srivastava, Selman Ipek, Cihan Okay, Shohini Ghose
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42017v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42017v1.pdf

Abstract:
We construct logical Bell inequalities whose violations are determined by the Wigner function of a reduced quantum state. For odd-prime qudits, each phase-space point of any nonempty proper subsystem defines an inequality, violated precisely when the corresponding Gross Wigner value is negative. This correspondence allows us to quantify the contextuality that a reduced state guarantees for the composite system. The normalized violation gives a lower bound on the contextual fraction in the complete Weyl measurement scenario, valid for every joint state with that marginal, irrespective of its correlations. We identify a family of product states that saturates this bound. For these states, one marginal Wigner value fixes the contextual fraction of the entire system.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.42029v1
- Title: Empirical Evaluation of Quantum Kernel Methods for Classification: A Comparative Study with Classical Kernels
- Authors: Ronak Bhalgami
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.LG
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42029v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42029v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum kernel methods are the most concrete near-term proposal for quantum machine learning on classical data, yet published benchmarks disagree on whether they outperform tuned classical kernels, and the disagreement cannot be resolved from the literature because the comparisons are not protocol-controlled. We argue that the obstacle is one of measurement, not theory: the usual setup fixes the quantum feature map's data scaling while grid-searching the classical kernel's hyperparameters, recomputes nothing per fold, and reports cost in simulator time. We therefore treat the comparison itself as the object of study and impose three controls. The bandwidth is tuned on the same grid budget as the classical $γ$, the kernel is recomputed inside every fold from fold-fitted preprocessing, and cost is counted in hardware circuit evaluations. On top of these we run a factorial design of 324 cells in which dimensionality, training size, class separability and data structure are explicit covariates, together with four standard datasets, a shots-versus-gate-noise ablation, and a cost sweep. The dominant effect we measure is not quantum: tuning the feature map's bandwidth is worth 0.120 accuracy on synthetic data and 0.339 on real data, exceeding every quantum-classical difference we observe. Tuned quantum kernels are then statistically indistinguishable from a tuned RBF on all four real datasets. The advantage on synthetic data is confined almost entirely to data generated by the feature map itself, and it shrinks with dimensionality (-0.0045 per qubit) while growing with sample size. Finite sampling costs an order of magnitude more accuracy than gate and readout noise, and a fidelity kernel needs $5\times10^{7}$ circuits at n = 10,000. Protocol variance is thus sufficient to explain the field's contradictory conclusions.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33

- Title: Benchmarking Single-Photon Sources for Measurement-Device-Independent Quantum Key Distribution
- Authors: Janka Memmen, Yvonne Lasars, Tobias Heindel, Nathan Walk, Anna Pappa
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42041v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42041v1.pdf

Abstract:
Measurement-Device-Independent Quantum Key Distribution (MDI-QKD) enables secure communication without trusting the measurement devices, but its performance critically depends on the quality of the employed photon sources. We compare MDI-QKD implementations based on Weak Coherent Pulses (WCPs) with those employing Quantum Dot (QD) single-photon sources. WCP-based schemes are fundamentally limited by Poissonian photon-number statistics, imposing a trade-off between single- and multi-photon emissions, whereas QD sources exhibit sub-Poissonian statistics with low antibunching parameter $g^{(2)}(0)$. Using a decoy-state analysis for both source types, we find that even at moderate mean photon numbers, QD-based implementations outperform the best-case WCP configuration - where the mean photon number is optimised at each transmission distance - across the full range of transmission distances. We further incorporate the effect of imperfect photon indistinguishability via Hong-Ou-Mandel interference visibilities into the security framework and demonstrate that both source types are highly sensitive to even small deviations from ideal indistinguishability, significantly affecting achievable key rates and maximum transmission distance.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.42069v1
- Title: The Quartic Gap Between Quantum and Dequantized Algorithms for Linear Algebra Is Tight
- Authors: Shouvanik Chakrabarti, Dylan Herman, Junhyung Lyle Kim, Guneykan Ozgul
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.DS; math.OC
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42069v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42069v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum algorithms for linear algebra that access a matrix $A$ through quantum random access memory (QRAM) prepare quantum states proportional to the solutions of linear systems and related problems. Dequantized classical algorithms, when given sampling and query access to $A$, return sampling and query access to an approximate solution. Both kinds of algorithms use a number of queries that is polynomial in the normalized Frobenius norm $ρ=\|A\|_F/\|A\|$ and does not directly depend on the dimension. For linear systems at fixed condition number and accuracy, quantum algorithms use $\widetilde O(ρ)$ queries, whereas the best dequantized algorithms use $\widetilde O(ρ^4)$. We prove that this quartic gap is tight in the worst case, up to a logarithmic factor. For every constant $κ_0>1$, we construct dense linear systems with condition number at most $κ_0$ for which sampling from the solution within a sufficiently small constant total variation distance requires $Ω(ρ^4/\logρ)$ classical queries but only $Θ(ρ)$ quantum queries. The same separation holds, for errors below smaller thresholds, for positive-definite systems, least squares, ridge regression, matrix powers and square roots, imaginary-time and real-time Hamiltonian evolution, and sampling from the top principal component. Our results therefore show that many quantum algorithms for linear algebra, achieve a near-quartic speedup in the worst case, over classical algorithms with sampling and query access. All of these classical lower bounds follow from the same framework, which is based on a lower bound for sampling from distributions that depend on a fixed set of Fourier coefficients of an unknown Boolean function.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.42114v1
- Title: Topologically Protected Edge States in a Single Flux Qubit
- Authors: Pablo Perez-Fernandez, Manuel Pino
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.mes-hall
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42114v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42114v1.pdf

Abstract:
We show that topological edge states can be realized in a simple superconducting circuit composed of an inductively shunted flux qubit. The Josephson potential of that qubit forms a honeycomb lattice similar to the case of graphene. The additional shunting element produces a quartic potential along one flux direction, which effectively creates a boundary in flux space. We map analytically the low-energy dynamics of the circuit to an extended Su--Schrieffer--Heeger (SSH) chain, in which a quartic potential is added to the usual SSH Hamiltonian. This mapping is confirmed by numerical simulations of the full superconducting circuit. We argue that chiral symmetry still holds for the edge states of the extended model around a chiral point in parameter space. Close to this point, we numerically find that edge states in our superconducting circuit are topologically protected against perturbations that obey chiral symmetry, as is the case for charge noise. Our results show that topological effects previously thought to occur in multi-qubit systems, such as protection against decoherence, can also be found in much simpler single superconducting qubits.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.42129v1
- Title: Weighted hypergraph states as a resource for quantum metrology
- Authors: B. J. Alexander, M. S. Tame
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42129v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42129v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum correlations can be used to improve sensing precision beyond classical limits, yet the correlated resource states needed can be challenging to prepare and preserve. Here, we study the family of weighted hypergraph states and identify subclasses characterized by their underlying graph topology that exhibit enhanced metrological performance. Using the quantum Fisher information and a novel Dicke state basis representation, we show that these subclasses outperform both separable and standard weighted graph state benchmarks over broad ranges of edge weights corresponding to weak controlled operations between qubits representing the vertices of the graph. We provide analytical formulas for an arbitrary number $N$ of qubits, supplemented by a general study, while focusing a detailed analysis on the example case of $N=4$. By identifying the critical probabilities for local dephasing, we show that adding higher-order weighted hyperedges for $N$ qubits can enhance metrological robustness, allowing the resulting states to retain a quantum advantage at higher noise levels. These probe states exhibit favorable scaling, enhanced precision, and noise tolerance. Our findings establish weighted hypergraph states as viable, structurally tunable and robust resources for quantum-enhanced metrology.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.42131v1
- Title: Time-optimal quantum dynamics under fixed-norm generators
- Authors: Oskar A. Prośniak, Karol Życzkowski
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; math-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42131v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42131v1.pdf

Abstract:
We investigate the dynamics of quantum systems whose evolution is governed by linear generators of fixed unitarily invariant norm. For this broad class of evolutions, we derive a universal time-optimal generator of the dynamics converting one given state into another. It provides a time-optimal non-Hermitian Hamiltonian for pure state conversion. We then use this general result to derive quantum speed limits for mixed-state evolutions governed by Lindbladians and non-Hermitian Hamiltonians. Our findings follow from the geometric properties of the group $GL(n,\mathbb{C})$, in particular the geodesic distance. The derived bounds hold for an arbitrary system size $n$, depend only on the norms of the evolution generator and the initial and final states, and do not require performing any time-averaging.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.42150v1
- Title: Secret-shared teleportation of a two-qudit gate with a deferred recipient
- Authors: Tanay Roy
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42150v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42150v1.pdf

Abstract:
An unknown quantum operation, like an unknown quantum state, cannot be perfectly copied or stored for later use. A specific gate can be embedded in an entangled resource and teleported, but its destination is fixed when the resource is created. Here we show that the capability to apply a two-qudit entangling gate can instead be secret-shared among many parties: the shares are distributed in advance, and a later key release lets exactly one of them, chosen after distribution, exercise it. A provider encrypts half of a Bell pair held with a user into $n$ shares, one per candidate node. The selected node, given the key qudits, completes an exact non-local $\mathtt{CSUM}^{k}$ gate with the user's data, in every measurement branch and for any qudit dimension, with the power $k$ chosen by the user alone. Any coalition of fewer than $n$ candidate nodes, or the key register alone, holds no information, unselected nodes cannot tell whether a selection occurred, and the key cannot decrypt twice. For two candidates the registers form a $((3,5))$ threshold scheme. Encoder and decoder are explicit $O(n)$ circuits. The construction supports jointly authorized and anonymous gate application, advancing distributed quantum computing and quantum network capabilities.

- Title: Gapless and Non-Equilibrium Topology in Blockaded Ladders
- Authors: Ramanjit Sohal, Hannes Bernien, Ruben Verresen
- Categories: cond-mat.str-el (primary); cond-mat.str-el; cond-mat.quant-gas; physics.atom-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40771v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40771v1.pdf

Abstract:
We propose a multi-leg PXYP model as a minimal setting to explore constraint-induced intertwinement between itinerant and internal degrees of freedom. We argue that the Rydberg-like blockade induces correlations in squeezed space, providing a unified mechanism for nontrivial string order both in and out of equilibrium. In a two-leg geometry, this leads to an intrinsically gapless topological ground state, which can terminate at a supersymmetric multicritical point. This mechanism also leads to the preservation of string order for parametrically long times after a quench, which we verify using tensor network simulations and a classical stochastic approximation to the dynamics. In a three-leg geometry, the blockade leads to an additional emergent $U(1)$ symmetry, resulting in a multicomponent Luttinger liquid where both itinerant and internal degrees of freedom are critical. This family of models arises naturally in a range of quantum simulator platforms such as Rydberg tweezer arrays and dipolar atoms in optical lattices.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40786v1
- Title: Singlet-Triplet Transition of Rotating Moiré Atoms in a Magnetic Field
- Authors: Conor Smith, Yubo Yang, Shiwei Zhang, Miguel A. Morales
- Categories: cond-mat.str-el (primary); cond-mat.str-el; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40786v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40786v1.pdf

Abstract:
Moiré superlattices can bind several electrons to each potential minimum, forming few-electron "moiré atoms." Using neural quantum states that obey magnetic periodic boundary conditions, include full Landau-level mixing, and treat both spin sectors, we map the ground state of two-electron moiré atoms in a superlattice as a function of moiré depth and magnetic field strength. At strong field each atom is a spin-triplet maximum-density droplet with canonical angular momentum $\langle L_z\rangle=-1$ and a persistent circulating current: the rotating counterpart of the "Wigner molecular crystal" recently imaged in moiré superlattices. Deepening the wells drives a singlet-triplet transition to non-rotating spin-singlet atoms whose only current is a weak diamagnetic counter-circulation. The transition moves to deeper wells as the field grows, so depth and field together open a window for the formation of rotors. This is the collective analog of the singlet-triplet transition of two-electron quantum dots. At the transition, the orbital magnetic moments of the atoms reverse sign as their spin polarization switches, a joint signature accessible to magnetometry. If the atoms are instead polarized by other means, such as magnetic proximity coupling, the rotors lock at weak field into a spontaneously ordered crystal of dimers in a pinwheel pattern.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40791v1
- Title: Relating approaches to interacting quantum clocks and systems
- Authors: Artur Semião, Leonardo Chataignier
- Categories: gr-qc (primary); gr-qc; hep-th; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40791v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40791v1.pdf

Abstract:
The problem of describing the dynamics of quantum systems relative to quantum clocks with which they interact is relevant to the physics of quantum reference frames and quantum gravity alike. There have been different approaches that derive a relational time-dependent Schrödinger equation for the system relative to the clock from a time-independent Hamiltonian constraint. We show that some of these approaches, due to Smith and Ahmadi, Gemsheim and Rost, as well as the so-called Born-Oppenheimer approach, can be unified in a single framework for describing conditional quantum states. In particular, the Born-Oppenheimer approach to quantum cosmology is seen to correspond to a type of interacting Page-Wootters formalism. We discuss the issue of unitarity and linearity, or lack thereof, in the relational dynamics, and we conclude with a brief outlook to further developments.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40851v1
- Title: Benchmarking One-Dimensional Quantum Lattice Boltzmann Methods on Quantum Hardware
- Authors: Melvina Quartey, Lucy T. Zhang
- Categories: physics.comp-ph (primary); physics.comp-ph; physics.flu-dyn; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40851v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40851v1.pdf

Abstract:
Classical computational fluid dynamics (CFD) methods encounter significant computational bottlenecks in large systems and at high Reynolds number flows due to the rapid increase in degrees of freedom, even on flagship supercomputers. As quantum computers emerge, the Quantum Lattice Boltzmann Method (QLBM) has been proposed as a quantum-native alternative for solving fluid dynamics problems. However, until recently, nearly all studies have relied on classical quantum simulators rather than real quantum hardware. This study presents a benchmark of a one-dimensional QLBM implementation executed on three different IBM Quantum hardware. We design and test a quantum circuit for the advection-diffusion equation using amplitude encoding, a Bhatnagar-Gross-Krook (BGK)-type collision operator, and a streaming step with periodic boundary conditions. Our numerical experiments evaluate transpiled two-qubit gate count, total circuit depth, runtime, and accuracy metrics including maximum absolute error ($L_\infty$ norm) and root-mean-square error (RMSE). We find that the present collision and streaming operators dominate the overall cost and become the primary scalability bottleneck on current hardware. To mitigate the prohibitive cost of full quantum state tomography on hardware-based benchmarking exercises, we introduce a sampler-based measurement strategy that reconstructs macroscopic density fields directly from computational-basis measurements. This approach substantially reduces measurement overhead while preserving the expected advection-diffusion dynamics on hardware. These results provide a quantitative assessment of QLBM performance in the noisy intermediate-scale quantum (NISQ) regime and identify the principal algorithmic and experimental limitations that must be addressed for QLBM to become viable for fluid dynamics.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40874v1
- Title: Proof of the entropic $c$-theorem
- Authors: Jonah Kudler-Flam
- Categories: hep-th (primary); hep-th; cond-mat.str-el; math-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40874v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40874v1.pdf

Abstract:
We prove an entropic $c$-theorem in two dimensions by constructing a $c$-function from the mutual information between spacelike separated wedges, defined through the relative entropy. The proof follows Casini and Huerta's, with strong subadditivity of entanglement entropy replaced by increasing differences of the mutual information, which we prove directly in the continuum from the split property and standard properties of relative entropy. The benefit of relative entropy is that every quantity we use is finite and no cutoff ever needs to be introduced. The assumptions of the theorem are only the standard axioms of algebraic quantum field theory, the split property with finite mutual information across a gap (both implied by modular $p$-nuclearity), and a finite ultraviolet bound on the resulting $c$-functions. We also note that, formally, the $c$-function is proportional to the minimal averaged null energy of a state that reproduces the vacuum correlations between the two wedges.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40952v1
- Title: Characteristics of VHF and UHF band detection using Stark shifting in Rydberg-atom sensors
- Authors: Rajavardhan Talashila, Noah Schlossberger, David S. La Mantia, Abby Halasi-Kun, Baran Kayim, Michael A. Viray, Robert Wyllie, Christopher L. Holloway, et al.
- Categories: physics.atom-ph (primary); physics.atom-ph; physics.optics; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40952v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40952v1.pdf

Abstract:
We present a characterization of the sensitivity of broadband electric field measurements in the Very High Frequency (VHF) and Ultra High Frequency (UHF) bands using AC stark shifting of Rydberg states. The dependence of the sensitivity and dynamic range of the field measurements using nD5/2 states of cesium for principal quantum numbers n= 60, 70, 80, 90 and 100 is presented, detailing the intricacies involved. The frequencies considered for the measurement range from 50 MHz to 900 MHz for which a transverse electromagnetic (TEM) cell is used to excite the Rydberg atoms with the RF fields. The corresponding sensitivities achieved are in the $(μV/m)/\sqrt{Hz}$ range. A general trend of better sensitivity for higher principal quantum numbers is observed. This work paves the way for a better understanding of the AC Stark dependent field measurements using Rydberg sensors.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40979v1
- Title: Microscopic Theory of Spin-Orbit Interactions of Light
- Authors: Saeed Rahmanian Koshkaki, Ding Xu, Michael Fowler, Milan Delor, Arkajit Mandal
- Categories: physics.optics (primary); physics.optics; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40979v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40979v1.pdf

Abstract:
Optical spin-orbit interactions (SOI), demonstrated experimentally in optical cavities, enable the polarization degrees of freedom of light to be described and controlled as an effective pseudospin, providing a foundation for spintronic-like control of light and the development of next-generation optical technologies. However, prior works describe this emergent effect using phenomenological models that fail to uncover its microscopic quantum origins, and as a result, a rigorous connection between material electronic structure and the optical SOI remains elusive. In this work, we address this fundamental challenge by developing a microscopic quantum theory starting from the non-relativistic quantum electrodynamical (QED) description of light-matter interactions. We show that this effect arises from virtual interactions between confined photons and material excitations. Crucially, our theoretical framework provides microscopic material-design principles for tuning the distinct components of optical SOI, which include the optical birefringence, photonic self-energies, TE-TM splitting, and optical Zeeman splitting, enabling precise engineering of effective magnetic fields, diabolical points, and pseudospin textures. We anticipate that the predictive capability of our microscopic theory will enable systematic screening and design of material systems for photonic spintronics and optical technologies.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40986v1
- Title: Exact Electron Dynamics in Linear and Chiral Cavities: Complete Field versus Single-Helicity Reduction
- Authors: Patrick Barron, Kalman Varga
- Categories: cond-mat.str-el (primary); cond-mat.str-el; physics.chem-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40986v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40986v1.pdf

Abstract:
Can a chiral cavity turn a free electron into a Landau-like orbiting particle? We answer with exact solutions of three quadratic electron--cavity models in the dipole limit, valid at arbitrary coupling. A single linearly polarized mode dresses the electron mass, which delays its flight, slows the spreading of a wavepacket and entangles the packet with the cavity. The complete transverse field, with both circular helicities, keeps two commuting, gapless translations at any coupling, mode number and helicity splitting, so the electron stays ballistic and no synthetic magnetic field appears. Keeping a single helicity gives two inequivalent models that are not related by a gauge transformation: reducing before the Power--Zienau--Woolley transformation gives ballistic motion, whereas reducing after it gives a Landau-like crossed-quadrature Hamiltonian. They differ by a real uniform field $B=sλ^2/(2ω_c)$ ($ω_c$ the mode frequency, $λ$ the coupling, $s=\pm1$ the helicity). In the complete theory the frequency $ω_{-s}$ of the partner helicity, measured against $g^2=λ^2/(2ω_c)$, decides between them: the electron is ballistic for a stiff partner, and Landau-like only at the soft-partner point, through an exact canonical reorganization that holds for the active sector but not for observables such as the electron--photon entanglement. Projecting onto one helicity after the PZW transformation is therefore not by itself a mechanism for synthetic magnetism; the mode content of the cavity is.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41003v1
- Title: Tunable Three-Body Interactions in Microwave-Shielded Polar Molecules
- Authors: Hanwei Yang, Tijs Karman
- Categories: cond-mat.quant-gas (primary); cond-mat.quant-gas; physics.atom-ph; physics.chem-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41003v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41003v1.pdf

Abstract:
We show that microwave shielding, which enables collisionally stable ultracold polar molecules, also leads to genuine nonadditive three-body interactions comparable to the shielding interaction itself. Their magnitude, sign, and geometrical dependence can be flexibly controlled through microwave parameters and orientation. Interaction strengths can reach the ten kilohertz-scale for ultrapolar molecules in typical optical lattices. The nonadditive three-body interactions can stabilize novel many-body phases, and should be accounted for in quantum simulation with microwave shielded molecules.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41138v1
- Title: Induced Lorentzian geometry from the fermionic quantum metric
- Authors: Patrick Copinger, Pablo Morales
- Categories: hep-th (primary); hep-th; cond-mat.mes-hall; gr-qc; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41138v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41138v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum geometry is conventionally understood in parameter or momentum space and need not be related to a geometry in configuration space. Here we show for Dirac fermions in a background electromagnetic field that an induced Lorentzian geometry is generated from the fermionic quantum metric. We study a Lorentz covariant phase space worldline path integral construction, whose application of the adiabatic theorem onto spin degrees of freedom leads to the appearance of the Berry phase at $O(\hbar)$, and the quantum metric at $O(\hbar^2)$ in the worldline action. Both the Feynman and retarded/advanced propagators are examined in 3+1 and 2+1 dimensions, whose different on-shell implementations lead to related quantum metrics; QED/QED${}_3$ and condensed matter Dirac theory are considered. An effective theory is found by integrating out the momenta degrees of freedom, which gives rise to an emergent Lorentzian metric in configuration space, whose perturbation to the flat metric is dependent on the quantum metric and the background field strength. Apart from the spin degrees of freedom encoded into the Berry phase, the effective theory describes a scalar particle moving in the induced quantum geometry. Phenomenological applications to the covariant geometry are further studied using spectral holonomy in a gapped 3-dimensional Dirac material.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41240v1
- Title: Longitudinal electric corner modes in continuum quantum gauge theory with matter
- Authors: Goncalo Araujo-Regado, Philipp A. Hoehn, Alok Laddha, Bilyana Tomova
- Categories: hep-th (primary); hep-th; gr-qc; math-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41240v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41240v1.pdf

Abstract:
Subregions in gauge theories give rise to edge modes and corner charges supported on their boundaries. While this is well-established classically and in quantum lattice gauge theories, the fate of corner modes remains poorly understood in continuum quantum field theories owing to their distributional nature. In this paper, we revisit this challenge and propose a new corner operator for a regional Cauchy slice algebra generated by local gauge-invariant observables of scalar QED in Minkowski spacetime. The corner mode is given by the longitudinal electric field sourced by the matter outside the subregion and thus constitutes a centre for the regional algebra. Our main goal is to show that there exists a perturbative GNS state on this algebra that quantises this mode. Pulling back the Fock vacuum to the slice, we construct this state by renormalising the divergences arising from the lack of temporal smearing. We clarify that the inclusion of the new centre mode necessitates a further renormalisation on the corner of the region, thereby physically distinguishing it and breaking the translation invariance of the state. Our work shows how the requirements of locality, renormalisability, and positivity together put strong constraints on the existence of a continuum quantum field theory which supports corner modes as genuine quantum operators. We finish with comments about the von Neumann completion of the regional algebra, as well as possible physical interpretations of the renormalised vacuum state.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41504v1
- Title: Quantum Popcorn: the shape of a phase transition bubble
- Authors: Kfir Blum, Ipak Fadakar, Omri Rosner, Guilherme L. Pimentel
- Categories: hep-ph (primary); hep-ph; gr-qc; hep-th; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41504v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41504v1.pdf

Abstract:
A quantum tunneling event in field theory is usually depicted as the materialization of a spherical bubble of true vacuum. The bubble appears at rest, and its profile is the time-symmetric slice of Coleman's bounce, the Euclidean solution that controls the decay rate. However, the bubble is spherical only in the leading semiclassical approximation: at the next order the configuration is a Gaussian ensemble of fluctuations around the bounce. We derive the wave functional of the field on the nucleation slice from the half-bounce path integral, and use it to compute the root-mean-square quadrupole moment of a bubble at nucleation. The relative deformation of the bubble wall radius $R$ is $δR/R\sim(\hbar/S_b)^{1/2}$, with $S_b$ the bounce action. Thus $δR/R\sim0.1$ for a typical cosmological phase transition with $S_b/\hbar\sim100$.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41523v1
- Title: A Hybrid Message-Passing and Electric-Field Representation for Electrostatic QM/MM Embedding with Machine Learning Interatomic Potentials
- Authors: Raphael T. Husistein, Shobhit S. Chaturvedi, Thomas Weymuth, Beatriz Piniello, Sélène Forget, Kresten Lindorff-Larsen, Gemma C. Solomon, Markus Reiher
- Categories: physics.chem-ph (primary); physics.chem-ph; physics.bio-ph; physics.comp-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41523v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41523v1.pdf

Abstract:
Within a hybrid quantum mechanics/molecular mechanics approach a small region of a large atomistic system is described quantum mechanically, while the rest of the system is represented by a classical force field. Despite this, the computational cost of the quantum calculation severely limits the accessible simulation and sampling time. Replacing the quantum calculation with a machine learning interatomic potential (MLIP) trained on quantum reference data can greatly increase the accessible simulation time. However, the accuracy and efficiency strongly depend on the specific way in which the MLIP incorporates information about the classical (electrostatic) environment. Here, we demonstrate that existing strategies based purely on features derived from an external electric field fail to distinguish certain environments, ultimately limiting their accuracy. As a solution, we propose an extension to the equivariant TensorNet architecture that mitigates this shortcoming by incorporating classical atoms close to the quantum region into the message-passing scheme, combined with features derived from the electric field for describing the environment farther away. We show that this combination achieves higher accuracy than either approach alone.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41657v1
- Title: Mathematical foundations of second quantization: an introduction through spin-boson models
- Authors: Davide Lonigro
- Categories: math-ph (primary); math-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41657v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41657v1.pdf

Abstract:
These lecture notes originate from a one-semester course on the mathematical foundations of second quantization, first given at the Department of Physics of the Friedrich-Alexander-Universität Erlangen-Nürnberg in the Winter Semester 2025/26 to a mixed audience of physics and mathematics students. Their guiding thread is a class of physical models: spin-boson models, describing a two-level system coupled to a boson field. After summarizing the necessary background on unbounded operators, up to the Kato-Rellich and KLMN theorems, and developing direct sums and tensor products of Hilbert spaces and of unbounded operators, we treat single-mode boson fields, constructing the ladder and number operators and characterizing their domains, before moving to Fock spaces, second quantization, bosonic and fermionic annihilation and creation operators, and the canonical (anti)commutation relations. Throughout, spin-boson models provide both the motivation and the test bench for the formalism: we rigorously establish their self-adjointness, solve the pure dephasing and Jaynes-Cummings models explicitly, deriving their spectra and the qubit channels they induce, show how their multimode counterparts display irreversible decoherence and decay, and, in an appendix, prove the rotating-wave approximation for the quantum Rabi model with an explicit error bound. Finally, we use the KLMN theorem to construct spin-boson models with non-normalizable form factors, and briefly discuss stronger ultraviolet divergences and the need for renormalization. Exercises with full solutions are included.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41788v1
- Title: Query-Efficient Quantum Multi-Objective Optimization for Materials Configuration Search via Bayesian Weight Selection
- Authors: Onur Boyar, Seiji Takeda, Akihiro Kosugi, Indra Priyadarsini S, Lisa Hamada, Sina Klampt
- Categories: physics.comp-ph (primary); physics.comp-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41788v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41788v1.pdf

Abstract:
Materials design is a multi-objective problem, and recent advances in quantum optimization make it possible to bring quantum solvers to bear on such problems. The realistic near-term win, though, is not raw quantum advantage but making an expensive, noisy quantum solver usable by spending its calls wisely. Quantum Approximate Multi-Objective Optimization (QAMOO) traces a Pareto front by solving a family of scalarized Ising problems, one per objective-weight vector, with QAOA --- but chooses those weights \emph{non-adaptively}, sampling them at random, blind to what it has already found. We recast weight selection as \emph{multi-objective Bayesian optimization}: our method, BW-QAMOO (Bayes-Weighted QAMOO), picks each next weight by expected hypervolume improvement (EHVI) under a Gaussian-process (GP) surrogate over the weight-to-objective map. On a frustrated alloy-configuration benchmark across $m\in\{3,4\}$ objectives and $n\in\{10,11,12\}$ sites, BW-QAMOO reaches the same Pareto hypervolume as QAMOO's random sampling with a median 33% fewer scalarized solves across the 90/95\% targets and 60--74\% fewer than a uniform-grid baseline), on a genuine (simulated) QAOA solver; a paired Wilcoxon test confirms the saving is significant, and the advantage grows with the objective count m as the weight simplex expands and blind sampling wastes more of the budget. This is a \emph{query-efficiency} result, and we close with the open challenges on the path to real alloy data and hardware.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41858v1
- Title: Channel dichotomies
- Authors: Aadil Oufkir, Mario Berta
- Categories: cs.IT (primary); cs.IT; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41858v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41858v1.pdf

Abstract:
We study channel interconversion under non-signalling superchannels, where the asymptotic conversion rate is given by the ratio of the channel capacities. Our main result determines the second-order expansion of the optimal target length. Namely, when each channel has a single capacity-achieving dispersion, the coefficient is given by the inverse sesquinormal distribution, with parameters determined by the capacities and dispersions. The result extends state-dichotomy theory to channels: one superchannel approximates the target uniformly over inputs and maps every reference channel whose output is independent of its input to another such channel. As in state dichotomies, there are finite-error resonances where the second-order term vanishes, leaving an $O(\log n)$ remainder. Crucially, our new characterization cannot be obtained by concatenating channel coding and simulation protocols [Cao {\it et al.}~IEEE Trans.~Inf.~Theory (2024)]. Instead, the proof combines comparisons of binary hypothesis testing experiments with uniform Gaussian estimates and a construction that uses symmetry within type classes to satisfy the non-signalling constraints. Shared randomness assistance already suffices at first order, but we show that its second-order behaviour can differ strictly from the non-signalling case.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41898v1
- Title: The Role of Electron-Nuclear Coupling on Chemical and Physical Processes
- Authors: Soumya Mukherjee, Satrajit Adhikari
- Categories: physics.chem-ph (primary); physics.chem-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41898v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41898v1.pdf

Abstract:
Over the last few decades, Born-Oppenheimer (BO) and Born-Oppenheimer-Huang (BOH) treatments for electron-nuclear equation of motion have been considered as the cornerstone for qualitative as well as quantitative exploration and understanding of chemical and physical processes. The BO approximation due to the large mass difference between electrons and nuclei as well as the energy separation among the electronic states could able to predict some of the processes (chemical and physical) satisfactorily at lower energy regime. Such approximation (BO) quite often fails for those cases with the involvement of excited electronic states, in particular, due to the coupling among the states. On the other hand, the BO treatment provides two important quantities, namely, the adiabatic potential energy surfaces (PESs) and the nonadiabatic coupling terms (NACTs) determining the forces, which control the motion of the nuclei in a molecule. The NACTs play a major role in photoexcitation spectra, charge transfer chemical reactions, ferroelectric property and other processes, where significant anomalies are observed between single and multiple PES(s) results. Since the NACTs often exhibit singularity at the close vicinity of degenerate states forming conical intersections (CIs), such coupling terms could break the symmetry of the molecular processes changing the observables qualitatively.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41917v1
- Title: Focusing through coupled multimode fibers for miniaturized single-atom tweezer arrays
- Authors: Benjamin P. Maddox, Daniel K. Ruttley
- Categories: physics.optics (primary); physics.optics; physics.atom-ph; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41917v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41917v1.pdf

Abstract:
Real-world deployment of quantum technologies requires systems in low SWaP-C packages. Quantum platforms based on ultracold atoms typically trap and control atoms with optical tweezers using light focused through bulky objective lenses, which are costly and sensitive to misalignment. Multimode fibers (MMFs) offer a compact route to focusing light, but their use in forming optical tweezers for cold atoms has been limited to a single fiber. Here we use a 2x1 MMF fused coupler to obtain two independent control paths sharing a common distal facet. We form near-diffraction-limited tweezers through each channel, suppress interference by applying a small frequency offset between the channels, and demonstrate that the tweezer separation is insensitive to misalignment of the input coupling. This work provides a route to reconfigurable single-atom arrays in low SWaP-C environments.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.41976v1
- Title: Solvable coupled cluster states in $d=\infty$ with prescribed momentum distribution
- Authors: Izak Snyman, Serge Florens
- Categories: cond-mat.str-el (primary); cond-mat.str-el; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41976v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.41976v1.pdf

Abstract:
Two of the most successful ideas in many-body theory, the exponential coupled cluster ansatz and DMFT, have to date developed separately. We establish a connection between them, by showing that the infinite dimensional limit of the hypercubic lattice allows the exact closed-form evaluation of static expectation values of coupled cluster wave functions, provided the cluster amplitudes admit a low-rank decomposition. As a result, evaluation errors -- a serious concern in conventional coupled cluster theory -- can be disentangled from limitations to expressivity of coupled cluster states. For this family of coupled cluster states, the cavity construction in $d\to\infty$ exactly reduces the environment of a lattice site to a Gaussian Grassmann kernel, and irreducible diagrams for one-body expectation values are found to be site-diagonal (in analogy to the DMFT self-energy). The resulting ``impurity problem" is an integral over a finite number of Grassmann variables rather than a dynamical quantum impurity problem, so that static observables derive exactly from a static self-consistency condition: a wave function counterpart of DMFT. For the simplest case of a cluster amplitude restricted to rank-one doubles, we obtain the momentum distribution in closed form. We show that any momentum distribution can be realized by a state that is the exact and unique ground state of an explicit parent Hamiltonian with a gapless single-particle tunnelling spectrum. By construction these coupled cluster states variationally recover low order perturbation theory for the Hubbard model. Their failure in the strong coupling regime near the Mott transition diagnoses a fundamental limitation of coupled cluster doubles. Intriguingly, our family of coupled cluster wave functions includes a gapless state with zero quasiparticle weight, with a momentum distribution that is completely smooth at the Fermi energy.

- Authors: Runze Chi, Meng Zeng, Tao Xiang, Yi-Zhuang You, Bertrand I. Halperin, Juven Wang
- Categories: cond-mat.str-el (primary); cond-mat.str-el; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42103v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42103v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantized charge pumping in quantum Hall systems is a bulk topological property, typically manifesting on gapless boundaries as spectral flow through chiral edge modes upon flux insertion. When the boundary is anomaly-free, it can be symmetrically gapped by symmetric mass generation (SMG) interactions. However, the effects on quantized charge pumping at an interacting gapped boundary are unclear. We address this using density-matrix renormalization group simulations of a four-leg ladder quasi-(2+1)d realization of the (1+1)d 3-4-5-0 lattice chiral fermion model with six-fermion interactions at one edge. We find gapless modes near the interacting edge for both weak and strong couplings, but the edge can be in a fully gapped SMG phase at intermediate coupling. We also identify spontaneous breaking of the model's $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry above a critical interaction strength. Across all three boundary phases, we find quantized charge pumping with the same pumped charge per flux quantum. The spatial distribution of the pumped charge varies between phases. Besides charge pumping, we study how a single injected fermion propagates under open boundary conditions. In the SMG phase, an interaction-induced flavor-mixing current develops at the endpoints of the gapped edge, which is absent in the weak- and strong-coupling limits. This current reveals a charge-redistribution mechanism unique to the SMG phase. Additionally, we propose an analogy between charge pumping in this model and a topological leptogenesis scenario in the (3+1)d Standard Model. The anomaly-free chiral U(1) symmetries of the 3-4-5-0 model play a role analogous to the baryon-minus-lepton symmetry of the Standard Model. In both settings, charge pumping can be associated with a continuous deformation along a classically defined but quantum-anomalous direction, while a discrete subgroup remains an exact quantum symmetry.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.42119v1
- Title: Functional Tensor Trains for High-Order Greeks and Exposure Tails of Multi-Asset Structured Products
- Authors: Javier Lopez-Piqueres, Atithi Acharya, Yue Sun, Niraj Kumar
- Categories: q-fin.CP (primary); q-fin.CP; math.NA; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42119v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42119v1.pdf

Abstract:
Risk management of worst-of autocallables needs price sensitivities (Greeks) up to third order and the potential future exposure, a tail quantile of the note's future value. In Monte Carlo, each order of differentiation multiplies the sampling noise by a factor that grows with the observation frequency, and nested simulation turns the noise of its inner paths into a bias of the quantile. We instead solve the backward pricing recursion, storing the continuation value as an $hp$ functional tensor train (hp-FTT) whose piecewise-polynomial cores are split at the barriers, so that worst-of barrier events act exactly, with rank one. For Gaussian transitions we show that the polynomial degree sufficient for an error $\varepsilon$ grows only as $\sqrt{\log(1/\varepsilon)}$. One solve then gives every Greek at any spot without sampling noise, by integrating the likelihood-ratio weight against the stored value, and the values it stores at future dates replace inner simulation. Against the strongest Monte Carlo estimators we could build or find published, on worst-of autocallables observed monthly on three to five assets, the order of the Greek and the accuracy decide which method is cheaper. At $1\%$ accuracy Monte Carlo is more than an order of magnitude cheaper for deltas, second-order Greeks go either way, and hp-FTT is cheaper for every third-order Greek, by up to two orders of magnitude. At $0.1\%$ hp-FTT is cheaper for every Greek we could check, under geometric Brownian motion and local volatility alike. For the exposure alone Monte Carlo is cheaper at $1\%$ and hp-FTT at $0.1\%$, and a solve run for the Greeks yields it for little more than scenario sampling. Sampling pays in variance for each derivative and hp-FTT in rank for each asset, so Monte Carlo keeps low-order Greeks at percent accuracy, rarely observed contracts and many assets.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.42120v1
- Title: Persistence of Unitary Almost Mathieu Operator Gaps Ten Dry Martinis, Five on the Rim
- Authors: Dan S. Borgnia, Matthew Faust, Long Li
- Categories: math-ph (primary); math-ph; cond-mat.mes-hall; math.SP; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42120v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.42120v1.pdf

Abstract:
We determine exactly which gap labels are realized by open spectral gaps for the unitary almost Mathieu operator at every irrational frequency and all coupling constants ($0<λ_1,λ_2\leq1$). When both couplings are strictly less than one, every permitted gap label is realized by an open gap, including in the critical case ($λ_1=λ_2$). When at least one coupling equals one, both lifts of every odd winding label are realized by open gaps, whereas all even winding labels are collapsed. The proof combines quantitative almost reducibility and Aubry duality with a monotonicity principle for gap heights.
