- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2410.04630v2
- Title: Unitary Closed Timelike Curves can Solve all of NP
- Authors: Omri Shmueli
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04630v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.04630v2.pdf

Abstract:
Born in the intersection between quantum mechanics and general relativity, indefinite causal structure is the idea that in the continuum of time, some sets of events do not have an inherent causal order between them. Process matrices, introduced by Oreshkov, Costa and Brukner (Nature Communications, 2012), define quantum information processing with indefinite causal structure -- a generalization of the operations allowed in standard quantum information processing, and to date, are the most studied such generalization.   Araujo et al. (Physical Review A, 2017) defined the computational complexity of process matrices, and showed that polynomial-time process matrix computation is equivalent to standard polynomial-time quantum computation with access to a weakening of post-selection Closed Timelike Curves (CTCs), that are restricted to be $\textit{linear}$. Araujo et al. accordingly defined the complexity class for efficient process matrix computation as $\mathbf{BQP}_{\ell CTC}$ (which trivially contains $\mathbf{BQP}$), and posed the open question of whether $\mathbf{BQP}_{\ell CTC}$ contains computational tasks that are outside $\mathbf{BQP}$.   In this work we solve this open question under a widely believed hardness assumption, by showing that $\mathbf{NP} \subseteq \mathbf{BQP}_{\ell CTC}$. Our solution is captured by an even more restricted subset of process matrices that are purifiable (Araujo et al., Quantum, 2017), which (1) is conjectured more likely to be physical than arbitrary process matrices, and (2) is equivalent to polynomial-time quantum computation with access to $\textit{unitary}$ (which are in particular linear) post-selection CTCs. Conceptually, our work shows that the previously held belief, that non-linearity is what enables CTCs to solve $\mathbf{NP}$, is false, and raises the importance of understanding whether purifiable process matrices are physical or not.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2502.12241v3
- Title: Certification of quantum correlations at arbitrary distances through routed Bell tests
- Authors: Pavel Sekatski, Jef Pauwels, Edwin Peter Lobo, Stefano Pironio, Nicolas Brunner
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12241v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.12241v3.pdf

Abstract:
Transmission loss represents a major obstacle to the device-independent certification of quantum correlations over long distances, limiting applications such as device-independent quantum key distribution (DIQKD). In this work, we investigate the recently proposed concept of routed Bell experiments, in which a particle sent to one side can be measured either near or far from the source. We prove that routed Bell tests involving only entangled qubits can certify quantum correlations even in the presence of arbitrary loss on the channel to the distant device. This is achieved by adapting concepts from self-testing and quantum steering to the routed Bell test framework. Finally, as a natural extension of our approach, we outline a DIQKD protocol that, in principle, is secure over arbitrary distances.

- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.15276v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.15276v2.pdf

Abstract:
We introduce a $0.611$-approximation algorithm for Quantum MaxCut and a $\frac{1+\sqrt{5}}{4} \approx 0.809$-approximation algorithm for the EPR Hamiltonian of [arXiv:2209.02589]. A novel ingredient in both of these algorithms is to partially entangle pairs of qubits associated to edges in a matching, while preserving the direction of their single-qubit Bloch vectors. This allows us to interpolate between product states and matching-based states with a tunable parameter.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2509.06584v5
- Title: Reaffirming a Challenge to Bohmian Mechanics
- Authors: Jan Klaers, Violetta Sharoglazova, Marius Puplauskis
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.06584v5  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.06584v5.pdf

Abstract:
In our work [Sharoglazova et al., Nature 643, 67-72 (2025)], we reported the first measurement of the speed of tunnelling particles by comparing two orthogonal motions in a coupled-waveguide system. Here we respond to objections raised in comments, referee reports, preprints, and articles. In particular, we distinguish two questions that are often conflated: whether Bohmian mechanics reproduces the measured density, and whether the standard guiding equation assigns the correct state of motion to the particles. The first point follows under the usual quantum equilibrium assumptions. By contrast, identifying the guiding velocity with particle motion is an additional physical postulate, since the standard guiding equation does not follow from the Schrödinger equation alone. We argue that, in the evanescent regime, the state of motion assigned by the standard guiding equation is in disagreement with the speed following from our experiments. To make the distinction explicit, we also present a bidirectional Bohmian model that reproduces the same stationary density while assigning finite speeds compatible with the speed inferred in the evanescent regime.

- Title: Explicit construction of a 2-design of ${\rm U}(2)$ from the theory of angular momentum
- Authors: Rocco Maggi, Cosmo Lupo, Saverio Pascazio
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; math-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17127v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.17127v2.pdf

Abstract:
The main aim of this work is to present an explicit construction of a 2-design of ${\rm U}(2)$, relying only on a tool that belongs to every physicists toolbox: the theory of angular momentum. Unitary designs are a rich and fundamental mathematical topic, with numerous fruitful applications in quantum information science and technology. In this work we take a peek under the hood. We begin with a minimal set of definitions and characterizations. Then we derive all 1-designs of ${\rm U}(2)$ of minimum size. Finally, we set out, step by step, a completion procedure extending such 1-designs to 2-designs. In particular, starting from the Pauli basis $\unicode{x2014}$ the prototypical unitary 1-design $\unicode{x2014}$ one $\unicode{x201C}$naturally$\unicode{x201D}$ obtains the 2-design originally employed by Bennett and coauthors in $\textit{Mixed State Entanglement and Quantum Error Correction}$. The present work also serves as a gentle and largely self-contained introduction to the subject.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2510.05384v2
- Title: Levitated optomechanics with cylindrically polarized vortex beams
- Authors: Felipe Almeida, Peter Barker
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.optics
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05384v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.05384v2.pdf

Abstract:
Optically levitated and cooled nanoparticles are a new quantum system whose application to the creation of non-classical states of motion and quantum limited sensing is fundamentally limited by recoil and bulk heating. We study the creation of stable 3D optical traps using optical cylindrically polarized vortex beams with radial and azimuthal polarization and show that a significant reduction in recoil heating by up to an order of magnitude can be achieved when compared with conventional single Gaussian beam tweezers. Additionally these beams allow trapping of larger particles outside the Rayleigh regime using both bright and dark tweezer trapping with reduced recoil heating. By changing the wavelength of the trapping laser, or the size of the particles, non-linear and repulsive potentials of interest for the creation of non-classical states of motion can also be created.

- Title: Witnessing Short- and Long-Range Nonstabilizerness via the Information Lattice
- Authors: Yuliya Bilinskaya, Miguel F. Martínez, Soumi Ghosh, Thomas Klein Kvorning, Claudia Artiaco, Jens H. Bardarson
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech; cond-mat.str-el
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.26696v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.26696v2.pdf

Abstract:
We study nonstabilizerness on the information lattice and demonstrate that noninteger local information directly indicates nonstabilizerness. For states with a clear separation of short- and large-scale information, a noninteger total information at large scales $Γ$ serves as a witness of long-range nonstabilizerness. We propose a folding procedure to separate the global and edge-to-edge contributions to $Γ$. As an example we show that the ferromagnetic ground state of the spin-1/2 three-state Potts model has long-range nonstabilizerness originating from global correlations, while the paramagnetic ground state has at most short-range nonstabilizerness.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2511.12258v3
- Title: Separation-Dependent Bell Correlations from Prepared Coherence in Dirac Wavepackets
- Authors: Ju Gao, Fang Shen
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.atom-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.12258v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.12258v3.pdf

Abstract:
We calculate separation-dependent Bell correlations for a fixed pair of Dirac electron wavepackets in a free, weakly relativistic packet model, treating the prepared joint wave as a physical wave--entity. Each station separation is sampled at the corresponding central packet arrival time. For the illustrated phase \(χ=π/4\), the magnitude of the prescribed-setting Bell--CHSH response decreases and revives; optimizing the spin measurement directions retains the modulation. It occurs already at leading spin order, including for equal prepared branch weights. Interference between direct and exchanged assignments of the electron waves to the stations changes the relative weights and phases of the sampled spin amplitudes, thereby modulating the optimized response. Representative finite spatial and timing windows reduce visibility while retaining broader suppression and revival. This wave--entity account gives a quantitative, testable correlation pattern from prepared coherence, deterministic propagation, and local sampling, without postulating transmission between detectors.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2512.12588v2
- Title: A $k$-entanglement measure complying with the many-body resource theory and its evaluation
- Authors: Jie Guo, Shuyuan Yang, Jinchuan Hou, Xiaofei Qi, Kan He
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.12588v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.12588v2.pdf

Abstract:
$k$-entanglement provides a hierarchical characterization of multipartite correlations according to their entanglement structure, ranging from multipartite entanglement ($k=n$) to genuine multipartite entanglement ($k=2$). Its quantitative treatment for mixed states faces three related obstacles: the standard axioms for such quantifications does not incorporate the requirements from many-body resource theory, as it   does not regulate how to quantify the entanglement assigned to part systems of the same multipartite state and how to describe the rule that the entanglement possessed by a part system never exceeds the entanglement possed by whole system;   no explicit measures are known that satisfy the above requirements from many-body resource theory; and the extreme difficulty in numerical evaluation at mixed states of known quantifications. In this work, we first formulate an axiomatic framework for \emph{true} $k$-entanglement measures that obeys the many-body resource theory, by supplementing usual faithfulness and LOCC monotonicity with two new ones: unification condition and hierarchy condition. Then, for every $2\leq k\leq n$, we construct a witness-based true $k$-entanglement measure $E_w^{(k,n)}$ without the convex-roof extension, which is valid for both finite- and infinite-dimensional $n$-partite systems. Finally, we derive a method of constructing finite-dimensional $k$-entanglement witnesses from positive operator and $k$-separable-state optimizations, design an evaluation scheme of $E_w^{(k,n)}$ based on pre-established database of $k$-entanglement witnesses sampled by positive operators. To check the feasibility of this computing scheme, we develop a software tool, which implements the computation of the approximation of $E_w^{(k,n)}$ for systems up to four qubits. Numerical tests show that the software   is sufficiently accurate and reliable.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2512.22521v2
- Title: Quantum Noise Spectroscopy of Nanoscale Charge Defects in Silicon Carbide at Room Temperature
- Authors: Jinpeng Liu, Yuanhong Teng, Yu Chen, Yixuan Wang, Sen Luan, Chihang Luo, Jun Yin, Hao Li, et al.
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.mtrl-sci
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.22521v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.22521v2.pdf

Abstract:
The nanoscale charge environment critically influences the performance of semiconductor devices, yet traditional characterization techniques usually lack the spatial resolution to resolve nanoscale charge heterogeneity and identify microscopic noise sources. Here, we address this critical gap by utilizing individual PL5 color centers in commercial silicon carbide as room-temperature, broadband (near-DC to GHz) quantum spectrometers. We report the first real-time, nanoscale observation of single-charge tunneling dynamics at room temperature in 4H-SiC by monitoring the random telegraph noise using optically detected magnetic resonance (ODMR). This capability enables an electrical noise imaging technique that shows distinct noise variations on different wafers. Employing dynamical decoupling and $T_1$ relaxation spectroscopy, we observe correlations of noise spectral density across frequency bands and obtain the nanoscale electron paramagnetic resonance (EPR) fingerprint of spin defects in SiC. Ultimately, through surface chemical engineering, we identify the native oxide layer as the main source of resolved electromagnetic fluctuations. This work establishes a versatile atomic-scale metrology platform bridging quantum sensing and semiconductor engineering, offering critical insights for optimizing third-generation semiconductor interfaces and advancing solid-state quantum technologies.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2603.28624v2
- Title: Quantum Riemannian Hamiltonian Descent
- Authors: Yoshihiko Abe, Ryo Nagai
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; hep-th
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28624v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2603.28624v2.pdf

Abstract:
We propose Quantum Riemannian Hamiltonian Descent (QRHD), a quantum algorithm for continuous optimization on Riemannian manifolds that extends Quantum Hamiltonian Descent (QHD) by incorporating geometric structure of the parameter space via a position-dependent metric in the kinetic term. We formulate QRHD in both operator and path integral formalisms and derive the corresponding quantum equations of motion, showing that quantum corrections appear in the action integral but they are suppressed at late times by the time-dependent dissipation factor. This implies that convergence near optimal points is controlled by the classical potential while quantum effects influence early-time dynamics. By analyzing the semiclassical equation, we estimate a lower bound on the convergence time and numerically demonstrate whether QRHD works as a quantum optimization algorithm in some examples. A quantum circuit implementation based on time-dependent Hamiltonian simulation is also discussed and the query complexity is estimated.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2605.00088v2
- Title: A Unified Framework for Locally Stable Phases
- Authors: Zhi Li, Raz Firanko, Timothy H. Hsieh
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech; math-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00088v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.00088v2.pdf

Abstract:
We propose a unifying framework for characterizing pure and mixed state phases of matter across equilibrium, non equilibrium, and metastable regimes. We introduce the concept of locally stable states, defined by the operational property that any local operation (including post selection) can be reversed by a local channel. We prove that local stability is equivalent to a state being short range correlated, defined by the decay of both correlations and conditional mutual information. We demonstrate that these properties are invariant under locally reversible channels, thus defining locally stable phases. Furthermore, we prove that local stability implies both the decay of a family of nonlinear correlators, including the fidelity correlator, and the decay of correlations in the canonical purification, thus bridging the gap between mixed and pure states. Along the way, we establish two results which may be of independent interest: we show that post-selection on locally stable (short range correlated) states can be implemented via local channels and that quantum Markov chains can be characterized by the local computability of nonlinear observables.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2605.00094v3
- Title: Graph-theory measures capture weak ergodicity breaking on large quantum systems
- Authors: Heiko Georg Menzler, Rafał Świętek, Mari Carmen Bañuls, Fabian Heidrich-Meisner
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech; cond-mat.str-el
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00094v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.00094v3.pdf

Abstract:
We study the onset of weak ergodicity violations in closed quantum many-body systems and focus on cases in which they occur through a transition that is controlled by a model parameter, a scenario called a weak ergodicity-breaking transition. This situation is realized in the Rosenzweig-Porter model and the quantum sun model, which we investigate here. Our analysis is based on representing quantum systems in Fock space and examining the short-time dynamics of density autocorrelation functions. From these, we extract proxies for decay rates for each individual basis state. As a first main result, we show that the distribution of such decay rates captures known weak ergodicity-breaking transitions in both models. As a second main result, we establish a connection between the decay rates of density autocorrelations and graph-theoretical measures. Concretely, we demonstrate that moments of the distribution of the recently introduced graph-energy centrality also detect the weak ergodicity transitions, which is inherited from the behavior of the decay rates. While most numerical tools are limited to small system sizes, the quantities studied in our work can be calculated analytically for large systems of many hundreds of sites and in some cases, even in the thermodynamic limit.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2605.06101v2
- Title: Syndrome resampling enhances quantum error correction thresholds
- Authors: Luis Colmenarez, Áron Márton, Markus Müller
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06101v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.06101v2.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum error correction (QEC) enables fault-tolerant quantum computation but requires operating quantum hardware at physical error rates below code-dependent thresholds, which remains challenging for current devices. We introduce syndrome resampling, a general method that increases QEC thresholds of any decoder and suppresses logical errors without additional hardware, decoding modifications, or code-specific assumptions beyond syndrome statistics. The method exploits the fact that syndromes with low probability are likely to lead to logical failure, therefore biasing syndrome averages towards most likely syndromes effectively increases logical fidelities. We establish a direct connection between the Rényi coherent information (RCI) and powers of the syndrome probability distribution, showing that resampling syndromes according to these powers combined with maximum likelihood decoding (MLD) realizes a family of optimal thresholds associated with phase transitions in the RCI. Numerical simulations of surface codes demonstrate that syndrome resampling substantially increases thresholds for both optimal and suboptimal decoders and reduces logical error rates by up to four orders of magnitude in experimentally relevant regimes. We further show that syndrome resampling can be effectively implemented from finite data and combined with decoding-based post-selection to achieve additional gains. Finally, applying the method to existing experimental QEC data yields up to two orders of magnitude reduction in logical error rates without requiring additional measurements. Our results provide a practical and decoder-agnostic route to improved logical fidelities in near-term QEC experiments.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2605.06633v4
- Title: Interpretable Machine Learning for Building Analytic Quantum Circuits: the Case of Diagonal Operators
- Authors: Matvei Fedin, Andrey Morozov
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; hep-th
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06633v4  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.06633v4.pdf

Abstract:
Machine Learning has become a useful instrument across many fields, but the opacity of typical ``black-box'' models limits their value for scientific discovery. In this paper we present a methodology that uses \emph{interpretable} Machine Learning to generate analytic mathematical hypotheses about a physical system, which can then be confirmed by a formal derivation. We demonstrate the methodology on the problem of decomposing unitary operators into elementary quantum gates. By training an interpretable model on circuits obtained numerically and analyzing its weights, we are led to a hypothesis for the analytic structure of the decomposition. Applied to the family of diagonal operators, this hypothesis yields a universal, analytic quantum circuit valid for an arbitrary number of qubits, whose $CNOT$ count scales as $\mathcal{O}(2^n)$, improving on the $\mathcal{O}(2.5^n)$ of standard numerical methods. The resulting decomposition is proved rigorously in a companion paper.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2605.27211v2
- Title: Time Crystals on Quantum Devices
- Authors: Gonzalo Camacho, Benedikt Fauseweh
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.str-el
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27211v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.27211v2.pdf

Abstract:
Time crystals are nonequilibrium phases of matter characterized by the emergence of temporal ordering, in which an interacting many-body system develops robust structure in its time evolution that is not trivially dictated by the external driving or environment. While related phenomena have long been studied in classical nonlinear systems, their realization in entangled quantum matter represents a distinct frontier. The theoretical understanding of discrete time crystals has substantially advanced, yet recent experiments using modern quantum devices and quantum processors reveal regimes beyond established paradigms. These developments call for an extended classification of time-crystalline phases according to both their stabilization mechanisms and their physical character, including discrete and continuous, closed and open, critical, topological, quasiperiodic, and controlled realizations. We review recent implementations of time crystals on quantum platforms and propose such a classification framework, identifying promising directions for the discovery of novel time-crystalline phases of matter.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2608.05595v2
- Title: How Much Reconstruction Does Quantum Machine Learning Need? Late Fusion of Uncoupled Quantum Subcircuits
- Authors: Prabhjot Singh, Adel N. Toosi, Rajkumar Buyya
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.DC; cs.LG
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05595v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.05595v2.pdf

Abstract:
Circuit cutting lets a large quantum neural network (QNN) run as independent subcircuits on small devices, but rebuilding its outputs by quasiprobability reconstruction carries a classical sampling overhead exponential in the number of cuts. This paper asks whether machine-learning tasks need this step. We replace it with late fusion: subcircuits are trained without cross-cut coupling, and a small classical head combines their local measurements at a cost constant in the cut count. To characterize the trade-off, we introduce a reconstruction dial $Q$, a physically executable budget that retains a fraction of the quasiprobability terms, and a cut-entanglement diagnostic that indicates, within a fixed scale, how much reconstruction a task needs (Spearman $ρ=0.59$ over $104$ runs). Across synthetic and standard datasets, uncoupled late fusion is equivalent to full reconstruction within a predefined margin of $0.02$ accuracy on most benchmarks and pooled over the controlled sweep, at exponentially lower cost; deficits of $0.02$-$0.04$ remain on the most cross-cut tasks. Fusion tops the dial's accuracy/cost frontier, is more robust to shot and device noise, and loses nothing as the coupled model's entangling depth grows. We state the exact condition under which fusion must lose accuracy, a decision-relevant cross-cut correlator, and realize it with entangled-state inputs; for classical inputs the register partition discards no information. We claim no advantage over classical machine learning.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.31007v2
- Title: Noise redistribution by stabilizer codes: an organizing view of concatenated three-qubit codes
- Authors: Long Huang
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31007v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.31007v2.pdf

Abstract:
We formulate stabilizer-code error correction as a code- and recovery-dependent transformation of the noise channel. For a fixed recovery map, syndrome projection produces a block-diagonal logical channel whose diagonal Pauli weights follow from code-induced error classes, with no physical Pauli twirl; concatenation is then iterated redistribution, with the dominant logical error guiding the next code. The bit-flip and phase-flip three-qubit codes with standard recoveries supply a closed-form benchmark, and logically inequivalent recoveries appear as a design degree of freedom for shaping the channel presented to the next decoder. For amplitude damping, the three-qubit rules select $C_2(\hatσ_y)C_1(\hatσ_x)$ and give $f_l=1-\mathcal O(γ^2)$, with an ideal-gate crossover at $γ_c\approx0.30$ ($f=1-1.72755γ^2$) and a favorable gate-count comparison with the five-qubit code under the same assumptions. Correlated noise requires joint class statistics, whereas coherent noise retains intra-class coherences that make the same transformation usable in reverse as a tomographic fingerprint; there the companion work proves local identifiability from a rank certificate evaluated inside the parameter domain, classifies the sole permutation-symmetry ambiguity as structural at any chain length, and closes it with one two-qubit marginal channel. The construction remains an analytic benchmark, not a universal prescription for an inner code beneath a surface code.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.34972v2
- Title: Membership in the cone of quantum states over time is NP-hard
- Authors: Matt Hoogsteder-Riera
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; math-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34972v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.34972v2.pdf

Abstract:
A state over time (stote) pairs a state $ρ$ with a channel $\mathcal{E}$ as the Hermitian, generally non-positive operator $(ρ\otimes\mathbf{1})\star J_\mathcal{E}$. Characterizing the convex cone $\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}$ these operators generate was posed as an open problem in Hoogsteder-Riera, Calsamiglia and Winter (2026). We exhibit a linear copy of the bipartite separable cone as an exposed face of $\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}$: for output systems of the form $B = A'R$ with $A'$ a copy of the input, there is an explicit witness $W_0\in\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}^*$ and an explicit injective linear map $L$ with $σ\in \mathrm{SEP}_{+}(A{:}R)\iff L(σ)\in\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}(A{:}A'R)$. Composing with the NP-hardness of separability shows that exact membership, weak membership, and weak optimization are NP-hard for $\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}$ and its dual. A quantitative stability bound makes the weak-membership reduction valid at inverse-polynomial accuracy. Thus polynomial-time membership tests do not exist unless $\mathrm{P}=\mathrm{NP}$. Independently of this complexity assumption, Fawzi's theorem implies that $\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}(A{:}A'R)$ admits no finite semidefinite representation when $d=\dim A\ge2$, $r=\dim R\ge2$, and $d+r>5$.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.35183v2
- Title: Complete characterization of internal-external entanglement trade-offs in two-mode Gaussian states
- Authors: Yasusada Nambu
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; gr-qc
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35183v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.35183v2.pdf

Abstract:
We characterize the exact trade-off between internal and external entanglement in asymmetric two-mode Gaussian states at fixed local symplectic eigenvalues. Internal entanglement is quantified by the negativity $\mathcal{N}_{A:B}$ between the two modes, while external entanglement is quantified by the negativity $\mathcal{N}_{AB:CD}$ across a Gaussian purification. Numerical sampling suggests the boundary structure of the attainable region in the $(\mathcal{N}_{AB:CD},\mathcal{N}_{A:B})$ plane, which we then determine analytically. Parametrizing the global symplectic spectrum yields a closed admissible interval for the remaining spectral parameter at fixed $\mathcal{N}_{AB:CD}$. We prove constructively that every point in this interval corresponds to a physical covariance matrix, implying that all intermediate values of $\mathcal{N}_{A:B}$ between the resulting sharp bounds are achievable. The upper bound is attained for opposite-sign correlations of equal magnitude, while the positive branch of the lower bound occurs when one global symplectic eigenvalue equals unity. We also identify two external-entanglement thresholds, derive a saturated monogamy-type inequality, and recover the symmetric-state limit. Because the external negativity depends only on the spectrum of the reduced two-mode state, the bounds hold for any purification, including non-Gaussian purifications.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.35797v2
- Title: The $\varepsilon$-smoothed Entanglement of Formation and its relation to the Moreau-Yosida approximation
- Authors: M. E. Shirokov
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; math-ph; math.FA
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35797v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.35797v2.pdf

Abstract:
We describe a family of convex continuous functions $E_{F,\varepsilon}$, $\varepsilon\in(0,1]$, on the set of states of a bipartite quantum system (consisting of finite-dimensional or infinite-dimensional subsystems) obtained by applying the Mora-Piani-Briegel construction of $\varepsilon$-measure (proposed in [Phys. Rev. A 109, 052413]) to the Entanglement of Formation (EoF) with the use of the trace norm distance in the role of basic metric. These functions are non-increasing in $\varepsilon$ and converge pointwise to the EoF as $\varepsilon\to0^+$. We conducted a detailed analysis of properties of the functions $E_{F,\varepsilon}$, using (among others) the relationship between the family $\{E_{F,\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in[0,1]}$ and the family $\{E^λ_F(ρ)\}_{λ>0}$ of Moreau-Yosida approximations of the EoF explored in [arXiv:2609.30246].   We obtain a variational expression for the functions $E_{F,\varepsilon}$ (which for $\varepsilon=0$ turns into the standard variational expression for the EoF). We also describe the weak subadditivity property of the functions $E_{F,\varepsilon}$ and analyse their continuity properties.   The semicontinuity bounds for the EoF (presented in [Lob.J.Math., 46, 2632-2658],[arXiv:2404.07978]) allow us to obtain easily computable upper bounds on the rate of uniform convergence of the function $E_{F,\varepsilon}$ to the EoF as $\varepsilon\to0^+$ on the sets of states with bounded rank/energy of one of the marginal states.   Applicability of the obtained results to other entanglement measures, in particular, to the relative entropy of entanglement, is discussed.   As an auxiliary result, it is shown that for any state of an infinite-dimensional bipartite quantum system, there exists a nearest separable state w.r.t. the trace norm distance. The non-uniqueness of such a state is also shown.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.37991v2
- Title: Reducing the cost of ancilla purification for Majorana quantum computation
- Authors: Piotr Ćwikliński, Łukasz Fojcik, Michał Studziński
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.str-el
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37991v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.37991v2.pdf

Abstract:
Braiding Majorana zero modes does not by itself provide a universal set of quantum gates. Additional operations can be supplied through ancillary states, whose preparation need not have the same protection as braiding. Here, we consider purification of the non-Gaussian ancilla used for an entangling gate in Bravyi's scheme. We keep his elementary braid-and-fusion comparison and change how its outputs are combined. An additional layer compares the product of the three stabilizer signs. The first accepted errors giving an incorrect output then require three faulty input blocks rather than two. As a result, the preparation error decreases cubically instead of quadratically, and the asymptotic raw-copy overhead is reduced. We also construct a preliminary sequence in which a processed block is paired with a fresh copy from the same noisy source. For independent inputs supported in the even-parity sector and ideal purification operations, this sequence establishes a sufficient distillability fidelity of 59.95 percent after free Gaussian twirling. At a common source fidelity, it reduces the expected cost of preparing an input for the recurrence by more than seven orders of magnitude relative to the balanced construction studied here. Finally, we examine imperfect purification operations in an effective syndrome-noise model. The additional layer leaves the leading operation-error coefficient unchanged, although a nonzero error floor remains.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.38028v2
- Title: Sample Complexity for Generative Learning of Quantum Measurement Data
- Authors: Shuhan Zhang, Yi-Zhuang You
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38028v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.38028v2.pdf

Abstract:
Generative learning of a quantum measurement distribution from finite data requires choosing a model capacity that balances representation accuracy against estimation cost. We develop a bias-variance framework that decomposes the expected population Kullback-Leibler error at model capacity $s$ and sample size $N$ as $E(s,N)=B(s)+V(s,N)$, with representation bias $B$ and a variance term $V$ capturing the mean estimation error. In the well-sampled regime, $V(s,N)\simeq[NC(s)]^{-1}$, and exponential or algebraic laws for $B$ and $C$ yield four scaling templates for the optimal error, from inverse powers of $N$ to inverse powers of $\ln N$. For local autoregressive models, the context radius sets the model capacity, and $B$ is the summed conditional mutual information lost by truncating the context. For a smoothed histogram learner, we derive an exact finite-sample estimation cost that accounts for rarely observed contexts and recovers the parameter-count law in the well-sampled regime. For transverse-field Ising Born distributions, the independently evaluated bias and estimation cost predict both the optimal context radius and the optimal error. In the well-sampled regime, the estimation cost grows exponentially with context radius. Its competition with approximately exponential bias decay in the gapped regimes predicts power-law error decay in $N$, while algebraic bias decay at criticality predicts asymptotic inverse-power decay in $\ln N$. Bulk-site results are consistent with the corresponding finite-sample predictions. These results connect the structure of quantum measurement distributions to the model capacity justified by finite data.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.38299v2
- Title: Schur--Weyl Reduction of Finite-Copy Precision Bounds for Multiparameter Quantum Estimation
- Authors: Jianchao Zhang, Masahito Hayashi
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38299v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.38299v2.pdf

Abstract:
In multiparameter quantum estimation, incompatible measurements can impose trade-offs among estimation errors. Understanding the precision attainable with a finite number of jointly measured copies is therefore important, but direct optimization becomes costly as the tensor product space grows exponentially. For independent and identically distributed (i.i.d.) copies, the quantum statistical model is invariant under permutations of the copies. We exploit this symmetry through Schur--Weyl duality to remove permutation multiplicity spaces while using their dimensions as weights. This gives a reduced statistical model equivalent to the original under arbitrary collective measurements. On this reduced model, we derive an exact conic formulation of the tight Cramér--Rao bound and a semidefinite program for its Nagaoka--Hayashi (NH) relaxation. For fixed single-copy dimension, the reduced operator representation grows polynomially rather than exponentially with the number of copies. To approximate the tight bound, we use an exact quadratic identity to correct finite-ray dual solutions and obtain rigorous upper and lower bounds. For fixed copy number and parameter dimension, sufficiently fine meshes admit feasible primal points, and dual solutions with bounded objective gaps have uniformly bounded compact-search radii. These results yield quadratic convergence in the projective covering radius. Numerical examples illustrate the quadratic scaling of the mesh correction term and the computational reach of the reduced NH formulation.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.39169v2
- Title: Resonance and Transition-Channel Competition in Cavity-Assisted Floquet-State Stabilization
- Authors: Zhuocheng Ma, Hongzheng Zhao, Liang-You Peng
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.other
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39169v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39169v2.pdf

Abstract:
Static coupling to a dissipative cavity has been proposed as a route to autonomously stabilize Floquet eigenstates. The cavity provides an energy-selective relaxation channel: when its photon energy matches a quasienergy difference modulo drive quanta, it mediates transitions between Floquet states, followed by irreversible photon loss. Here, we show that at intermediate driving frequencies, this resonance condition alone is not sufficient for stabilization. Instead, the steady state is controlled by the interplay between resonance selection and the cavity-induced transition strengths between Floquet states, which can either cooperate or compete. This interplay leads to successful stabilization, mixed steady states despite resonance, and complete breakdown near Floquet-zone hybridization. We further show that the same mechanism extends to multilevel spin-S systems, providing a general framework for understanding when static dissipation can reliably stabilize driven quantum states.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33

- Title: Cross-Platform Verification of Continuous-Variable Quantum Systems
- Authors: Kun Wang
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39912v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39912v2.pdf

Abstract:
Cross-platform verification compares output states of independently operated bosonic and quantum optical systems when classical predictions and joint measurements are unavailable. Continuous-variable distributed inner product estimation (CV-DIPE) provides a local measurement approach, but infinite-dimensional state spaces require rigorous control of truncation error. We develop three CV-DIPE protocols for the natural class of energy-constrained continuous-variable quantum states. One uses coordinated displaced parity, another coordinated Haar measurements, and the third independent homodyne shadows. For arbitrary multimode inputs in this class, including entangled and non-Gaussian states, we establish truncation error bounds and worst-case sample complexity guarantees for the exact inner product. The leading terms of these bounds favor coordinated Haar measurements, whose global implementation is experimentally challenging. These protocols provide certified strategies for cross-platform verification of continuous-variable systems using only local measurement data.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2610.40561v2
- Title: Grow, Compress, and Perturb: A Hybrid Quantum-Classical Pathway to Scalable Quantum Chemistry on Quantum Computers
- Authors: Hugo D. Nogueira, Siwar Badreddine, Olivier Adjoua, Diata Traore, Guillaume Michel, Jean-Philip Piquemal
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; physics.chem-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40561v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.40561v2.pdf

Abstract:
While Variational Quantum Eigensolver (VQE) variants are among the leading candidates for electronic structure calculations on quantum computers, gradient-based operator selection limits their scalability. To solve this, we introduce the Hamiltonian-gradient-free VULCAN (Variational Upbuilding, Layered Compression, Adaptive N-body perturbation) layered VQE framework. Our method dynamically generates ansatz operators using Variational Double Bracket Flow (VDBF) equations. Combined with a linearly scaling minimal complete pool, it creates a pool 4-12 times smaller than QEB (Qubit-Excitation-Based) pools. By appending entire layers simultaneously, Hamiltonian gradient measurements are completely eliminated. Circuit depth is managed via a grow-then-compress scheme using efficient state-overlap measurements, and qubit requirements are reduced by projecting the Hamiltonian onto a dominant-determinant subspace. Using high-performance emulation up to 32 logical qubits, we achieve chemical accuracy at just 5-20% of the measurement cost of batched ADAPT-VQE for hydrogen chains, BeH2 and H2O. For the 32-qubit C3H6, where batched ADAPT-VQE does not reach chemical accuracy, a single VULCAN layer combined with a GPU-accelerated stochastic Epstein-Nesbet (ENPT2) correction does. This stochastic ENPT2 post-processing step enables early termination at chemical accuracy.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2406.10027v2
- Title: Resonant analogue configurations in atomic condensates
- Authors: Juan Ramón Muñoz de Nova, Pablo Fernández Palacios, Pedro Alcázar Guerrero, Ivar Zapata, Fernando Sols
- Categories: cond-mat.quant-gas (primary); cond-mat.quant-gas; gr-qc; physics.optics; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10027v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.10027v2.pdf

Abstract:
As a contribution to a memorial volume, we provide a comprehensive discussion of resonant configurations in analogue gravity, focusing on its implementation in atomic condensates and combining review features with original insights and calculations. In particular, we jointly analyze the analogues of the Andreev and Hawking effects using a microscopic description based on the Bogoliubov approximation. We perform a detailed study of the thermality of the Andreev and Hawking spectra for canonical black-hole solutions, finding that both can be described by a gray-body distribution to a very good approximation. We contemplate several resonant scenarios whose efficiency to enhance anomalous scattering processes is compared to that of non-resonant setups. The presence of quantum signatures in analogue configurations, such as the violation of Cauchy-Schwarz inequalities or entanglement, is analyzed, observing that resonant configurations highly increase the entanglement signal, especially for the Andreev effect. We also discuss how these results have served as inspiration for the rapidly expanding field of quantum information in high-energy colliders. Finally, we study the physics of black-hole lasers as further examples of resonant analogue structures, distinguishing three stages in its time evolution. For short times, we compute the linear and non-linear spectrum for different models. For intermediate times, we generalize the current analysis of the BHL-BCL crossover. For long times, we discuss the emerging concept of spontaneous Floquet state and its potential implications.

- Title: Quantum Batteries in two-dimensional material-based Josephson Junctions
- Authors: V. Varrica, G. Gemme, F. M. D. Pellegrino, E. Paladino, M. Sassetti, D. Ferraro
- Categories: cond-mat.mes-hall (primary); cond-mat.mes-hall; cond-mat.supr-con; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22582v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.22582v2.pdf

Abstract:
We investigate the solid-state implementation of a Dicke-like quantum battery consisting of a two-dimensional material-based Josephson junction inductively coupled to a resonator, using graphene as a representative example. In this configuration, Andreev bound states naturally act as non-interacting, energetically non-degenerate two-level systems, and the setup allows for both single-photon and two-photon resonant processes. The coupling between the LC-circuit flux and the supercurrent through the junction gives rise to peculiar longitudinal interaction terms that have no counterpart in the conventional Dicke model. We demonstrate that these additional couplings can enhance energy storage for a proper range of parameters. The proposed architecture also enables an alternative, but comparable, charging protocol that relies on tuning the superconducting phase difference across the junction.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-02 15:33
- arXiv: 2605.23734v2
- Title: The Floquet-Magnus expansion of unbounded operators
- Authors: Daniel Burgarth, Robin Hillier, Davide Lonigro, Leonhard Richter
- Categories: math-ph (primary); math-ph; math.FA; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23734v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.23734v2.pdf

Abstract:
The Floquet-Magnus expansion is a widely used tool to derive effective descriptions of time-periodic quantum systems by approximating their dynamics with a time-independent Hamiltonian. However, its standard formulation is, strictly speaking, restricted to bounded Hamiltonians. In this work, we extend its definition and analysis to a broad class of time-periodic unbounded Hamiltonians. Our approach is based on an a priori distinct nonperturbative framework for the construction of effective Hamiltonians, which we show to reproduce the Floquet-Magnus expansion. A particular strength of our framework is that it allows us to prove that the resulting effective dynamics approximates the original time evolution propagators to arbitrary order in the high-frequency limit without requiring convergence of the Floquet-Magnus expansion, a condition that is already highly restrictive even in the bounded setting. We illustrate the scope of the method on representative models: the quantum Rabi Hamiltonian in the interaction picture, and the periodically driven quantum harmonic oscillator.

- Authors: Sanjay Suresh, Caitao Zhan, Joaquin Chung, Rajkumar Kettimuthu
- Categories: cs.NI (primary); cs.NI; cs.DC; quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39476v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.39476v2.pdf

Abstract:
Distributed quantum computing (DQC) connects multiple quantum processing units to overcome the scale limits of monolithic hardware. We introduce SeQUeNCe$.$DQC a new top-level module within the SeQUeNCe package that compiles monolithic circuits into topology-aware execution plans and coordinates their execution using SeQUeNCe's existing quantum-network modules and protocols. We validate SeQUeNCe$.$DQC by reproducing a two-node trapped-ion Grover experiment in simulation and comparing success probabilities with the reported 71% benchmark. We then evaluate how compiler strategy, topology, and memory capacity jointly govern distributed circuit execution time and success rate. In these experiments, our hybrid compilers successfully identified mixed TeleGate/TeleData execution plans that outperformed pure gate-teleportation strategies. By capturing the physical overhead of distributed quantum primitives, SeQUeNCe$.$DQC provides a rigorous platform for co-designing DQC software and hardware architectures.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-04 15:47
- arXiv: 2004.10173v2
- Title: Everlasting secure key agreement from a quantum computational hybrid security model
- Authors: Nilesh Vyas, Romain Alleaume
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2004.10173v2  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2004.10173v2.pdf

Abstract:
We introduce the Quantum Computational Timelock (QCT) model, combining time-lock encryption with a quantitative noisy-quantum-storage assumption. Time-lock encryption gives Bob immediate access to the encoding seed and gives Eve the same final information as access to that seed only after a protected interval. Eve may then process her retained information without computational restriction. We state this delayed-access encryption assumption explicitly and construct MUB-QCT from a complete set of mutually unbiased bases in dimension $d$ and an affine pair-wise independent permutation family.   For single-copy encoding, every seed-independent immediate measurement has trace-distance advantage at most $1/(2\sqrt{d+1})$ and mutual information at most $1/(d+1)$. Effective residual storage at diamond distance at most $δ$ from replacement adds at most $δ$ to the individual-attack distinguishing bound. Under i.i.d. individual attacks and entropy-preserving acceptance, we obtain an asymptotic key-rate bound and formulate final-key secrecy in composable trace distance.   For $m$ independently randomized signals sharing one bit and seed, the optimal leakage under non-adaptive product measurements is $\min\{1,m/(d+1)\}$, allowing a linear-in-$d$ detection enhancement in that attack model. We also construct a seed-independent joint measurement that recovers the bit after disclosure with error at most $\varepsilon$ from $O(\log d+\log(1/\varepsilon))$ signals, retaining only classical information after measurement. This gives a quantitative separation between product and joint measurements. Bob uses two logical detection outcomes independently of $d$.

- Title: Perturbation-based Non-perturbative Method
- Authors: Chang Liu, Wen-Du Li, Wu-Sheng Dai
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.10996v3  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.10996v3.pdf

Abstract:
This paper presents a nonperturbative method for solving eigenproblems. This method applies to almost all potentials and provides nonperturbative approximations for any energy level. The method converts an eigenproblem into a perturbation problem, obtains perturbation solutions through standard perturbation theory, and then analytically continues the perturbative solution into a nonperturbative solution. Concretely, we follow three main steps: (1) Introduce an auxiliary potential that can be solved exactly and treat the potential to be solved as a perturbation on this auxiliary system. (2) Use perturbation theory to obtain an approximate polynomial of the eigenproblem. (3) Use a rational approximation to analytically continue this approximate polynomial into the nonperturbative region.

- Title: Dynamically modulated light-matter interactions for ancilla-tolerant bosonic control
- Authors: Aniket Maiti, John W. O. Garmon, Daniel K. Weiss, Takahiro Tsunoda, Yao Lu, Steven M. Girvin, Robert J. Schoelkopf
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05460v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05460v1.pdf

Abstract:
Strong light-matter interactions are central to state-of-the-art quantum technologies, spanning platforms as diverse as Rydberg atoms, trapped ions, and superconducting circuits. In superconducting circuits, the strong dispersive coupling between microwave photons (light) and ancillary qubits (matter) has enabled universal bosonic control and quantum error correction. However, this always-on dispersive interaction propagates errors from the ancillary qubits to the bosonic systems both while idling and when performing operations, introducing a trade-off between control strength and fidelity. We break this trade-off by introducing a parametrically activated hybridization between the bosonic modes and ancilla qubits, keeping both elements decoupled when not active. When activated, this hybridization mediates a dynamic light-matter coupling with non-trivial engineered symmetries, permitting resilience to ancilla decay and dephasing. This dynamic coupling functions as a new control primitive that offers a route to bosonic processors whose performance is set by the oscillator, rather than the ancilla.

- arXiv: 2611.05462v1
- Title: Spectral measures and sharpness of quantum speed limits
- Authors: Ivica Smolić
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; hep-th; math-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05462v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05462v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum speed limits bound the time required for a given state to evolve into a given subspace of the Hilbert space under specific energy constraints. We prove that for several standard orthogonalization time bounds, the unique spectral measure associated with the initial vector that saturates the bound is a sum of two Dirac measures. Furthermore, we constrain the families of spectral measures that come arbitrarily close to these bounds and construct examples with measures which are purely absolutely continuous and purely singular continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure. As a complementary perspective, we discuss the geometric intuition behind quantum speed limits provided by the numerical range of the evolution operator.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05466v1
- Title: Toward a Quantum Information Foundation for Neutron Scattering
- Authors: Michael J. Landry, Yongqiang Cheng, Daniel Pajerowski, Joshua J. Turner, Mingda Li
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.mtrl-sci; hep-th; nucl-th
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05466v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05466v1.pdf

Abstract:
Neutron scattering is a central probe of quantum materials, yet what quantum information can, in principle, be extracted from a scattering experiment remains unclear. Here we formulate neutron scattering as a quantum measurement, with the scattering amplitudes acting as Kraus operators on the material state. This provides a unified description of conventional intensity, coherent scattering, interferometric measurements, and entanglement witnesses, including those based on quantum Fisher information. We then ask how far this connection can be extended to nonlinear properties of a quantum state. For two independently prepared samples, we derive the general structure of probe-mediated measurement probabilities and show that, for spin-projection-conserving dynamics, low-order scattering cannot resolve certain many-body coherences required to determine purity. Conversely, an idealized single-spin protocol shows how probe-mediated exchange can access purity when the appropriate two-copy operation is realized. Our results establish a quantum-information description of neutron scattering and delineate the information accessible through conventional and interferometric measurements.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05471v1
- Title: Noise-Induced Topology in Chiral Quantum Walks
- Authors: Alexandre Chaduteau, Derek K. K. Lee, Frank Schindler, Abhinav Prem
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.mes-hall; cond-mat.str-el
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05471v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05471v1.pdf

Abstract:
Noise typically destroys quantum coherence and washes out transport phenomena that rely on interference. Chiral quantum walks are a case in point: a particle hopping on a lattice threaded by a magnetic flux can spread asymmetrically due to interference between paths, an asymmetry that noise is expected to erase. Remarkably, we find that the most ubiquitous noise -- random fluctuations of on-site energies (dephasing) -- instead renders this asymmetry topologically robust. The density profile develops a skewness that grows in time while its center stays fixed, and in a finite chain the late-time relaxation modes pile up at one edge: a skin effect lacking the drift of conventional non-Hermitian skin effects. With multiple sites per unit cell, the dephasing strength alone can cause a topological phase transition, reversing the asymmetry. On a flux ladder with noisy rung hopping, the noise causes a drift of rung coherences, a quantum ratchet with no classical counterpart. Underlying these effects, the noise-averaged dynamics is an interacting (generically non-integrable) Lindbladian that remains analytically tractable. The modes governing late-time relaxation are bound states of the noise-induced interaction and form a band with a point gap, absent both without noise and under postselection, whose winding number fixes the direction of the asymmetry. These effects are within reach of several experimental platforms, including synthetic lattices, superconducting qubits, and optical lattices.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05479v1
- Title: Fermionic Porter-Thomas statistics and their emergence in random matchgate circuits
- Authors: Guglielmo Lami
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05479v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05479v1.pdf

Abstract:
For Haar-random quantum states, measurement probabilities follow the universal Porter--Thomas distribution. This implies anticoncentration, i.e.\ the spreading of probability over exponentially many outcomes, a key property underlying random-circuit-sampling experiments. We derive the exact analogue of the Porter--Thomas law for random fermionic Gaussian states, both with and without particle-number conservation. We also consider Clifford Gaussian states, for which the problem maps onto a simple model of arcs and loops. For large systems the probabilities become log-normally distributed, with moments growing polynomially in system size. Free-fermion states thus anticoncentrate only up to polynomial factors. We then show how this fermionic Porter--Thomas law emerges in random one-dimensional brickwork matchgate circuits. A simple single-arc approximation accurately describes the full distribution of probabilities, revealing an early diffusive regime followed by a slow relaxation on times scaling as the square of the system size. We rigorously prove that depths growing almost quadratically with system size are required for the collision probability to converge. Finally, we show that ideal free-fermion sampling yields large linear cross-entropy scores, and propose a log-based benchmark with an exact fermionic normalization.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05481v1
- Title: Zero noise extrapolation of transport coefficients
- Authors: Hansveer Singh, Pieter Claeys, Sarang Gopalakrishnan, Anushya Chandran
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05481v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05481v1.pdf

Abstract:
NISQ devices routinely estimate properties of the noiseless system from measurements at varying noise strengths $γ$. These zero-noise extrapolation (ZNE) schemes fail to estimate transport coefficients of conserved quantities when the noise violates the associated conservation laws. We show how to extrapolate measurements in noisy, one-dimensional, non-integrable spin chains to obtain the spin transport coefficients governing linear response in the zero-noise limit. As the wavevector of the Fourier mode of a conserved quantity goes to zero, its decay rate vanishes. The coefficient of the leading momentum dependence defines a transport coefficient in the noiseless system, and an analogous effective coefficient in the noisy one. Naive ZNE implicitly assumes that this effective coefficient is analytic in $γ$ as $γ\to 0$. Our main result is that universal non-linear corrections to hydrodynamics can produce non-analytic dependence on $γ$. In particular, we argue that the effective spin diffusion coefficient of a dissipative Heisenberg chain and the effective subdiffusion coefficient of a dipole-conserving fracton approach their noiseless value as $γ^{1/2}$. We benchmark these analytical predictions numerically.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05483v1
- Title: Classical training of conditional instantaneous quantum polynomial-time Born machines
- Authors: Oriol Balló-Gimbernat, Emiliano Tolotti, Laia Domingo, Fernando Vilariño, Adan Garriga
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.LG
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05483v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05483v1.pdf

Abstract:
Instantaneous quantum polynomial-time (IQP) Born machines are attractive quantum generative models, as certain IQP circuit families are conjectured to be hard to sample, yet they can be trained classically. Previously proposed conditional IQP Born machines make the parameters of the diagonal layer condition dependent. We introduce conditional IQP Born machines, in which the condition is encoded in the input state and a single IQP unitary is shared across conditions. Efficient sampling and amplitude-ratio evaluation for the reference state entering the diagonal layer suffice to obtain unbiased classical estimates of a conditional maximum mean discrepancy objective and its gradients. We show that input conditioning can represent conditional families that no diagonally conditioned IQP model with the same number of qubits can reproduce. For the sixteen-qubit Curie-Weiss spin model, a single conditional model captures the magnetization transition at held-out temperatures. Its mean total variation distance is six times lower than that of diagonal conditioning under the same protocol and comparable to that of classical generative models with up to seven times as many parameters. We also train conditional models classically with up to 2048 qubits, which reproduce the mean squared magnetization of the target away from the crossover. These results extend classically trained IQP Born machines to conditional generation.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05495v1
- Title: Krylov meets Fock: Complementary lenses on quantum dynamics
- Authors: Giuseppe Di Giulio, Dimitrios Patramanis
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech; hep-th
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05495v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05495v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum dynamics in Krylov space can be represented by a particle hopping on a chain whose sites are the Krylov basis vectors. The goal of this work is to extend this idea to the Fock basis, which in turn allows the description of a generic dynamics in terms of a more general, possibly higher-dimensional lattice. This construction has been dubbed the Fock state lattice (FSL), since its sites are identified with Fock states. To analyze the dynamics on the FSL we introduce various measures of the spread of a state, comparing them among themselves and with their Krylov counterparts. We apply our analysis to two models of light-matter interaction studied in quantum optics: the multi-mode Jaynes-Cummings and the Dicke models. We find that, whenever the FSL is not a chain, the corresponding dynamics provides insights complementary to the Krylov space analysis and, in some instances, greater analytical control. For example, we obtain analytical results for the FSL spread measures of the Dicke model in the thermodynamic limit and determine that their scaling in time detects the superradiant phase transition.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05497v1
- Title: Boosted Entanglement Swapping
- Authors: Matthias J. Bayerbach, Simone E. D'Aurelio, Stefanie Barz
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05497v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05497v1.pdf

Abstract:
Entanglement swapping entangles photons that never interacted directly. When implemented with linear optics, its success probability is upper bounded by $50\%$, due to the limited efficiency of linear-optical Bell-state measurements. Here, we demonstrate boosted entanglement swapping using an ancilla-assisted linear-optical Bell-state measurement, which raises the theoretical success probability to $62.5\%$ and allows all four Bell states to be identified. We measure an acceptance rate of $p_{\mathrm{a}}^{\mathrm{b}}=(69.39\pm0.39)\%$ with an average fidelity of the swapped states of $\langle F^{\mathrm{b}}\rangle=(76.6\pm1.0)\%$. We implement this protocol using photons at 1550 nm, making it well suited for quantum repeaters, where it could enable more efficient entanglement distribution. Beyond quantum communication, it could also enhance fusion-based quantum computing.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05499v1
- Title: Raychaudhuri-Ehlers Kinematics on Quantum State-Space: Caustics and Extrinsic Quantum Geometry
- Authors: Neellohit Karmakar, Suraj S. Hegde, Aryan Bhatia
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cond-mat.stat-mech; gr-qc; math-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05499v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05499v1.pdf

Abstract:
Quantum mechanics is inherently geometric in that the pure states form a curved complex manifold and the Schrödinger evolution defines a Hamiltonian flow on this space. Geometry identifies constraints on flows on quantum state-space that hold independently of their microscopic realization. We uncover such universal behavior in families of geodesic paths on the quantum state-space using the Raychaudhuri-Ehlers equations. We show that the complex structure leads to tidal anisotropy in the curvature, and for Hilbert space dimension $N\geq3$, complete point-emitted geodesic bundles necessarily develop shear and undergo direction-selective focusing at finite distance. We give these geometric results an information-theoretic interpretation in terms of fidelity susceptibility and quantum Fisher information. For ground-state manifolds constrained by physical parameters, we resolve the tidal curvature into Berry-curvature-dependent ambient tides and contributions from extrinsic bending within the full ambient state space. We further relate extrinsic bending to variations in the unitary generator of motion along intrinsic geodesics. We present exact many-body geometric results for the transverse-field XY spin chain. The product structure of the ground state leads to an exact cancellation of the ambient tide, including its Berry-curvature enhancement, by extrinsic curvature contributions. At the zero-field Ising point, we obtain exact results at finite size and show that the shear source for anisotropy-directed motion is entirely extrinsic relative to the full many-body state-space. For the metric in the thermodynamic limit, the geodesic families exhibit repeated $A_2$ fold catastrophes inside the ferromagnetic phase. Our framework thus separates universal state-space effects from those of physical restrictions and exposes their consequences in a paradigmatic many-body system.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05502v1
- Title: Bounded decoding complexity in transversal circuits with magic state injection
- Authors: Lukas M. Sieberer, Giacomo Giudice
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05502v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05502v1.pdf

Abstract:
We characterize the complexity of space-time correlated decoding for fault-tolerant quantum computing architectures that implement Clifford gates transversally and realize non-Clifford gates via magic state injection. In this approach, decoding graphs for logical measurements are constructed by backpropagating measurement operators through the circuit, which causes them to grow with circuit volume. However, the decoding volume can be substantially reduced by decoding measurements sequentially, committing corrections, and excluding syndrome data that have already been processed for earlier measurements. For random circuits, we find that the resulting decoding volume associated with mid-circuit measurements approaches a stationary average that is determined by the density of magic state injections. Large decoding instances are exponentially suppressed. We further find that the decoding complexity is remarkably insensitive to different choices of measurement products to be decoded, and that the same qualitative behavior persists across different circuit connectivities and Clifford gate ensembles. These results suggest that scalable space-time correlated decoding of large-scale fault-tolerant quantum algorithms is achievable since the magic states that make transversal architectures universal also keep them decodable.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05509v1
- Title: From Claws to Conferences: Device-Independent Key Agreement Beyond Non-Signaling
- Authors: Suvradip Chakraborty
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; cs.CR
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05509v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05509v1.pdf

Abstract:
Device-independent conference key agreement (DI-CKA) allows a group of users to establish a common secret key without trusting the internal operation of their quantum devices. Existing DI-CKA protocols derive their security from multipartite Bell tests and therefore require the device components not to communicate during each round. We present, to the best of our knowledge, the first DI-CKA protocol that removes this non-signaling assumption: all interfaces and their mutual communication are treated as one untrusted device, which only has to be quantum polynomial-time while the protocol runs. Assuming that the Learning With Errors (LWE) problem is hard for quantum algorithms with quantum advice during the execution, we prove finite-size security against coherent attacks, in which the device may keep quantum memory and correlate and adapt its behavior across rounds. For two parties the protocol is a computational DI-QKD protocol with an explicit finite-size proof against coherent attacks.   Technically, we lift the claw state underlying the classical-verifier test of Brakerski et al.\ (FOCS 2018) to multipartite entanglement, obtaining a \emph{GHZ-correlated claw state}. %that is prepared with one coherent claw-free function evaluation and $N-1$ CNOT gates. We then introduce the \emph{collective adaptive hardcore bit}, a multiparty generalization of the adaptive hardcore bit of Brakerski et al.\ and show that it follows from the original property by a tight reduction. Combined with Jordan's lemma, an entropic uncertainty relation, and entropy accumulation, this certifies smooth min-entropy of the key against an external quantum adversary. Our protocol achieves \emph{everlasting security}: LWE needs to hold only during the execution, because the final key is statistically secure and its secrecy persists even if LWE is broken after the protocol ends.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05526v1
- Title: Quantum error correcting codes from quiver moduli: Grassmannians, cyclic monodromy, and argyle collision divisors
- Authors: Steven Rayan, Evan Sundbo
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph; hep-th; math.AG; math.CO; math.RT
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05526v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05526v1.pdf

Abstract:
Building on aspects of a recent diagrammatic field-theory programme for quantum error correction put forward by the first-named author (arXiv:2607.08911), we construct exact finite-dimensional codes from twisted cyclic and argyle quiver moduli on the projective line. A regular fibre of the node-labelled cyclic Hitchin map records the ways to distribute simple spectral zeroes among the arrows with prescribed degrees. The Hilbert space spanned by these allocations is a Young permutation module. Root monodromy acts on its Specht factors and leaves their multiplicity spaces noiseless. Resetting the Specht factor therefore recovers the encoded state for every channel in the monodromy noise model. We prove that the largest Kostka multiplicity is the optimal dimension of a subspace code correcting the full monodromy algebra, even among codes spanning several isotypic blocks. Quantizing an argyle master space recovers the cyclic sheet space as a weight sector of a tensor power, with the identification preserving monodromy. For the balanced three-cycle with $t$ zeroes per arrow, the multiplicity of a partition $(a,b,c)$ of $3t$ is $1+\min(a-b,b-c)$. The optimal logical dimension is thus $t+1$, attained uniquely by the block indexed by $(2t,t)$. At $t=1$ we give explicit codewords for a qubit on six sheets. The commuting right action generates its full logical algebra, yielding errors outside the monodromy model that leave the measured footprint statistics unchanged yet require different recoveries. We also compute collision-parity ranks, give a higher-rank Grassmannian example, and derive a conditional criterion for recovery across splitting types.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05537v1
- Title: Quantum theory of damping: A new approach to temporal evolution of density operator without the rotating wave approximation
- Authors: A. Marashi Nasab, E. Ghasemian, M. K. Tavassoly
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05537v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05537v1.pdf

Abstract:
The Lindblad master equation is the standard framework for describing dissipative dynamics of the open quantum system context. Its conventional derivation, however, relies on the rotating-wave approximation (RWA), which neglects the counter rotating terms in the system-reservoir interaction. Here, we derive a generalized Lindblad master equation beyond the RWA within the density operator formalism, retaining the full systemreservoir coupling. We consider two prototypical open quantum systems, a two-level atom and a single-mode quantized field, each interacting with thermal or squeezed reservoirs described by the Wigner-Weisskopf theory. The resulting master equations are applied to the evolution of representative physical observables and compared with the conventional RWA based formulation. We show that counter-rotating terms produce significant modifications to the dissipative dynamics and therefore cannot, in general, be neglected. For a thermal reservoir, increasing the mean thermal photon number, nth, enhances the decay of hσ^yi beyond the RWA but suppresses it within the RWA. Likewise, the evolution of hσ^zi exhibits opposite damping behavior with increasing Γt and squeezing parameter r. These findings demonstrate the importance of including counter rotating terms for an accurate description of open quantum systems.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05541v1
- Title: Higher-order quantum information inequalities and error-correction of non-Markovian noise
- Authors: Matheus Capela
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05541v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05541v1.pdf

Abstract:
Defining entropy of a quantum channel has become a challenge with potential candidates recently reported in literature. In this Article, it is taken under consideration the entropy of a quantum channel defined as the quantum entropy of its associated Choi state. The consequences presented here of such approach is two-fold. First, the limitations on the information-processing of quantum channels are assessed, leading to the \emph{higher-order quantum information inequalities}. Those are then considered as useful witnesses of non-Markovian effects beyond a given number of quantum side-channels in a quantum process. Importantly, the presentation is given with the application of the methods for the study of examples. Second, such approach provides a characterization on the condition for the recovery of quantum channel preparations under non-Markovian noise. Such result is a straightforward extension of standard error-correction conditions which gives further meaning to the higher-order information measures defined here.

- Date (JST ingest): 2026-11-05 16:07
- arXiv: 2611.05561v1
- Title: SPAM-robust learnability of quantum dynamics
- Authors: Zitai Xu, Guoding Liu, Senrui Chen
- Categories: quant-ph (primary); quant-ph
- Links: abs=https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05561v1  pdf=https://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.05561v1.pdf

Abstract:
Accurate characterization of quantum dynamics is essential for the calibration and control of quantum devices. Unknown state preparation and measurement (SPAM) errors, however, introduce gauge ambiguities that limit what can be learned. A central question is therefore which properties are learnable despite SPAM errors, and whether physical structure in the dynamics can restrict these ambiguities and expand the set of learnable properties. In this work, we address both questions within a unified framework for quantum channels and continuous-time evolution. First, for general quantum channels and Lindbladian dynamics, we use the cycle structure of a generalized pattern-transfer graph to fully characterize all learnable parameter combinations and show achievability by constructing explicit SPAM-robust learning protocols, establishing a baseline where continuous gauge freedom prevents many individual parameters from being identified. Second, we show that coherent structure drastically restricts this gauge ambiguity, reducing continuous gauge freedom to discrete or even trivial choices. This restriction manifests across both discrete- and continuous-time settings, including purely Hamiltonian evolution, noisy quantum channels close to a target unitary, and Lindbladian dynamics close to a target Hamiltonian. In particular, when the dynamics preserves or remains close to a coherent Hamiltonian form, all coherent coefficients and broad classes of dissipative error parameters become individually learnable. These results establish a systematic route to exploiting physical structure for SPAM-robust characterization, providing a foundation for the reliable calibration and control of quantum devices.
